# Research digest for *The Names We Carry*: the George Douglas Papers as supplied

Prepared 20 September 2026 from the annotated transcription (version 1.0) in this repository, checked against the independently prepared family reading edition. Source: George Douglas Papers, 1830–1939, Center for Restoration Studies MS 5, Abilene Christian University Special Collections and Archives; six supplied PDFs (387 scan pages), covering the finding aid, the 1883 sermon notes, the 1888 endorsement and manuscript folders 1–3 only. **Folders 4–7 (including N. L. Douglas's day book, the Civil War journal, the land certificate, the photographs, George's notebook and poems, and the 1863 letters) were not supplied and are not represented here.**

## How to read this digest

- Every statement is cited to a transcript block (for example `05-095-P01` = source PDF 05, scan page 25, block 1; source 04 is Folder 1, 05 is Folder 2, 06 is Folder 3). The same locators appear in `editions/annotated/Supporting-files/Complete-transcript.txt`, `Transcripts.json` and the HTML reading copy.
- Quotations are verbatim from the transcript, with its own uncertainty markers: `[[word?]]` tentative reading, `[[illegible]]` unread, `[[lost: …]]` damaged, `[[obscured: …]]` obstructed, `[[deleted: …]]`/`[[inserted: …]]` cancellations and insertions. Nothing inside a quotation has been silently smoothed. The register `Unresolved-readings.tsv` and `review/uncertain-text-review-2026-09.md` hold suggested readings for the marked words.
- Later identifying slips (written by an unknown family hand, probably in the twentieth century) are quoted as slips, never as manuscript evidence. Where a slip and the manuscript disagree, both are given.
- Dates are given as written, followed by an ISO form in brackets when the year is known.
- "Inference" marks anything not stated on the page. "The papers are silent" means the supplied scans contain nothing on the point.
- No scan image was consulted in preparing this digest (the PDFs are not in the repository and could not be obtained in the session); the transcription and the family edition are the sources.

---

## A. Inventory of every document in the supplied scans

Entries follow scan order within each source PDF. "Pages" are PDF scan pages counting the cover as 1. Repository covers and later identifying slips are listed with the documents they accompany.

### Source 01 — Finding aid (6 pages)

**A-01. Finding aid, ACU Special Collections, 16 January 2019 (01-001 to 01-006).** Printed repository description, 6 pages. Creator George Douglas; 1 linear foot in 2 boxes; dates 1830–1939; accession R 2007.45, accessioned 18 May 2007 by David Sessions and Julia Reid, processed 29 January 2014, finding aid by Audrey Schaffner and Mac Ice 30 January 2014, updated by Mac Ice and Amanda Dietz 16 January 2019. Scope note names a Civil War journal on the battle of Sabine Pass, a land grant certificate from the governor of Texas, two photographs of Douglas, N. L. Douglas's day book from 1830, loan and legal papers and family letters. Lists seven folders with a subject index of 30 names. Folder 1: correspondence and 1939 loan papers; Folders 2–3: correspondence and legal papers (item list 01-005-P05 to 01-006-P01); Folder 4: songs by Emily Douglas, Rebecca Goodloe's letters to her daughters, Miriam Stevenson to Mary Douglas; Folder 5: the day book "of Murfreesboro TN and Ellis County, Liberty Settlement, Red Oak TX beginning Jan. 4, 1830"; Folder 6: wartime letters 1863–1864 including George to Lizzie and Lizzie to George and Warren 28 Jan 1863; Folder 7: poems, an essay "What are social parties?", and a copy of George's personal notebook. Source of acquisition unknown.

### Source 02 — L. Douglas sermon notes (4 pages)

**A-02. Sermon notes, "Jesus wept", signed "L. Douglas. Sylvana Feb 16th 1883" (02-001 cover; 02-002 slip; 02-003 to 02-004 manuscript).** Two manuscript pages, a discourse on John 11:35 that cites Renan's *Life of Christ*, ending with a four-line hymn stanza "Forever reign victorious King". The slip reads "Sermon notes / Sylvana Church / (Brushy Creek) / Feb 16, 1883 / L(?) Douglas". First word and a torn edge damaged. Duplicated in Folder 2 at 05-077 to 05-079.

### Source 03 — Sylvana Church endorsement (5 pages)

**A-03. Endorsement of the Sylvana congregation for George Douglas, 2nd Lord's day in September 1888 (03-001 cover; 03-002 slip; 03-003, 03-005 manuscript; 03-004 docket).** Certificate that "Bro. George Douglas, who is a member of the Church of Christ worshiping at Sylvana Ellis County Texas, has for some length of time been laboring in word and doctrine" and goes "with our full permission & hearty approval", quoting 1 Corinthians 9:16 and the charge to Timothy. Signed by the elders H. Douglas and H G Orr. The slip and the finding aid both date it 1883; the manuscript reads 1888. Duplicated in Folder 2 at 05-074 to 05-076.

### Source 04 — Folder 1, correspondence and loan papers (96 pages)

**A-04. Letter, [[J. H. Scott?]] (cousin) at Orange Co., Texas, to "Cousin George", 28 June 1864 [1864-06-28] (04-002), with address panel "George Douglas Co C [[illegible]] Regt Sab Pass, By Mr Richardson" (04-003).** The writer's regiment has left Alexandria, Louisiana; he was detailed to Red River; reports the death of Uncle [[Alfred?]] on 5 June from a wound received 18 May. Heavily faded, upside-down scan.

**A-05. Letter, Henry (cousin) at Brushy, Ellis County, to "Mr George Douglas, Dear [[Bro?]] & Cousin", Friday 29 January 1864 [1864-01-29], with postscript Saturday 30th (04-004 to 04-007; scans out of sequence, reading order 4, top of 5, 7, 6, rest of 5).** Home after twenty-odd months; visits to Aunt Bettie's and Liberty; teasing about girls; his company's movements as conscript-catchers under Col Burleson in Hopkins County, Tyler, Shreveport, Bonham, Crockett, San Augustine; brigade ordered to Hempstead by 10 February; news of Jim Lowder of Ross's regiment, James wounded at Chickamauga, Capt Bledsoe, militia sent to Galveston; address "Hempstead Tex Co G Browns Regt". Very heavy photocopy interference.

**A-06. Two torn fragments of wartime letters (04-008, 04-009).** Scraps mentioning Col Patton, "a force of 2500", "the siege", Charleston, the blockade, "the Vicksburg affair", quibbling about who must go, "[[Yankee?]] is certain". Writer, date and relation to A-05 unknown.

**A-07. Letter, Henry (cousin) at Camp [[Yavely?]], Texas, to Cousin George, 13 March 1865 [1865-03-13] (04-010 to 04-011).** Condolence on the death of George's sister; camp moved 5 March, forage running out; Lafayette's unexpected arrival after the Tennessee campaign; Brother James's letter enclosed; Mat Brawner's transfer; "Kind Regards to Warren & John".

**A-08. Manuscript copy of the poem "The River of Death" ("Beautiful world! I've trusted long"), seven stanzas, with covering note from James at Bloomsdale to Cousin Lizzie, 6 July 1865 [1865-07-06], and title panel "Ballad 'The River of Death' Written for Lizzie" (04-012 to 04-014).** James copied the poem he had recited on the night of the 4th [of July].

**A-09. Two torn address fragments (04-015, 04-016).** "[[illegible]] Prairie … Apr 10 … Mr Henry Seale, Nashville Milam, Texas"; and "Nashville Texas Ap[…] Miss Lizzy Douglas, Redoak Po, Texas".

**A-10. Letter, Henry (cousin; signed "H D") at [[Yellow?]] Branch to Cousin George, 18 September 1863 [1863-09-18] (04-017 to 04-018).** Home on a clothing detail; the Claunch murder trial at Waxahachie; the Red Oak camp meeting; his command's summer in Louisiana, Natchez and Arkansas ("We had Seen the Elephant in La"); the surprise of 28 August; home news (Lizzie, John, Laura, Mother sick, Sam Austin); letter from Lafayette: regiment went into the fight of 22 July with 320 and came out with about 80, James wounded.

**A-11. Letter, "Bob" (initials R D G) at Cold Springs, Texas, Co. F, to "Mr George Douglas, Dear cousin", 19 September 1863 [1863-09-19] (04-019 to 04-020).** Old hunting companion and schoolmate on "old Brushy"; nearly four years since they met; camp life, a chain guard, a big meeting with forty mourners; brothers east of the Mississippi.

**A-12. Letter, James at Tupelo, Mississippi, to "Dear Brother", 25 January 1865 [1865-01-25] (04-021 to 04-024; scans 23–24 repeat parts of 21–22).** Wounded 22 July [1864] in the left wrist and hand charging the enemy's works; fever; furlough to Mobile, stopped at Demopolis with Mrs Brassfield; hospital at Columbus, Mississippi; brigade down to about 400 of five regiments, four of old Co. I fit for duty; Brother Lafayette going home on 75 days' furlough; Squire; "our cousins (the Jetton boys)", Jeppe a prisoner, Billy and Charly well; "the Six Texas Boys". Signature "James [[illegible]]".

**A-13. Envelope, Geo' Douglas, Red Oak, to Zella Douglas, 1715 Grand Avenue, North Fort Worth, postmarked Red Oak, Aug [day and year illegible], with pencil note on the reverse "Met today Anna Conger now Mrs Smithson Eva's old playmate at Ferris" (04-025, 04-026).** A second, similar envelope at 04-031.

**A-14. Letter, "G. D." (George Douglas) to "Dear Zella", Monday morning, undated, continued "At Kate's" and at Red Oak (04-027 to 04-030).** The meeting at Sylvana with dinner on the ground; visitors from Ennis, Oak Cliff and Waxahachie; Bro. Dunn the revivalist; Lucile's and Larkin's illnesses at Kate's; "The Ellis Co. cotton crop is a failure today the eleventh of August"; batching; eggs; baptisms of Ila Duncan and Mayfaire; Jesse Ligon's return; the old wooden centre of Red Oak burned. Signed "Affectionately G. D."; "My peach preserves were highly praised".

**A-15. Letter, G. D. to Dear Zella, "Wednesday Eve", undated (04-032 to 04-033).** Stayed with Clarence & Kate; saw Zella in the car window; a puncture fixed at Rockett with Sid Farrar; Kate's children look bad; the letter from Paris; Aunt Nannie; boiled potatoes and eggs; Ruth's plum jelly; Elmer Carter and the cow; Edwina's letter.

**A-16. Letter, G. D. to Dear Zella, "Tuesday Morning" (corrected from Monday), undated, with postscript "At Kate's Tuesday Eve" (04-034 to 04-035).** Ed's expenses; the housekeepers left for Bartlett, Texas; baching; the hens and the feterita patch; small meeting crowds; Olaf asks for baptism; Dr. Stoker gives him days to live; Lucile sits up an hour a day.

**A-17. Letter, G. D. to Dear Zella, "Thursday Morning" and "Red Oak Thursday Evening", undated (04-036 to 04-037).** Olaf died at six thirty; Larkin's blood poisoning from a hen scratch; Mrs. Wadley's death; Olof's burial at the Rawlins cemetery, Bro Dunn's talk at the tabernacle; Hattie Stanford to be baptized; Patrick Chapel and Oak Cliff visitors.

**A-18. Letter, G. D. to Dear Zella, "Thursday Morning", undated (04-038).** Still baching; fruit jars to Mary Roberts and Edna; a gallon and a quart of peach preserves from 12 lb peaches and 9 lb sugar; $13 of potatoes sold; Kate and children.

**A-19. Envelope, typed, from Suite 1614, 100 East 42nd Street, New York, to Mr. C. C. Douglas, 212 East University, Waxahachie, postmarked New York 28 February 1941 [1941-02-28] (04-039).** Outside the collection's stated dates; no enclosure survives.

**A-20. Federal Land Bank of Houston, Division of Commissioners Loans, closing statement for the loan of Bruce Headrick, Red Oak, Ellis association, 27 January 1939 [1939-01-27] (04-040).** Application 189553, loan 336394J, second lien, $1,000, note dated 11 January 1939 for 10 years, first payment $7.00 interest due 1 April 1939; check 341819 payable to the association, Bruce Headrick and Zella Douglas; "To pay balance purchase money lien"; attorney Thos. F. White.

**A-21. Federal Land Bank of Houston closing statement for the loan of Bruce Headrick, Route 1, Red Oak, through the Ellis N.F.L.A., 27 January 1939 (04-041).** Loan 92466J, $1,800 on 70 acres, A. J. Porter survey, Abstract 829, Ellis County; 18 shares of stock $90; loan fee $7; check 341818 for $1,703 payable to the Sec.-Treas. and Zella Douglas; payments $66.60 semi-annually; total indebtedness not to exceed $2,800; tax certificate through 1938; R. D. Johnson, Treasurer; application fee $17.

**A-22. Quitclaim deed, the heirs at law of N. L. Douglas to one another (docketed "Geo Douglas et al to Mary Douglas, Deed"), Ellis County, 19 February 1875 [1875-02-19], with acknowledgments 26 February 1875 (Ellis County, [[S. M.?]] Hancock) and 9 March 1875 (County of [[Rusk?]], [[Jeppe?]] Douglas), filed 21 August 1875 and recorded 23 August 1875 in Deed Record O pages 2–3 by J M Phillips, Clerk District Court, deputy H. C. Wise (04-042 to 04-045; scans 43–44 overlap).** Signed Jeppe Douglas, George Douglas, Lizzie Douglas, Laura Robinson, Thos. J. Robinson, Susanna Bell, J. M. Bell, J. H. Douglas, Warren Douglas. Recites that "Mary Douglas our mother" has deeded portions of the estate to each heir.

**A-23. Typescript field notes for Bruce Hedrick, 70.0 acres of the A. J. Porter Survey, Abstract 829, undated [with the 1939 loans] (04-046).** Begins at the NW corner of the Miss Zella Douglas property on the south line of the Geo. Douglas 80 acre tract (Deed Records Vol. Z, p. 58) and the NE corner of the J. H. Hedrick 20 acres (Vol. 179, p. 362); runs to the centre of Brushy Creek and up its channel.

**A-24. Letter, "Papa" at Union City [California] to "My Dear Child" (Zella), 3 September 1923 [1923-09-03], six pages, with envelope fragment "Sept 3 — Union City, Zella" (04-047 to 04-054) and envelope postmarked Union City, Ca., 7 September 1923 (04-055).** Instructions to sell the Ford for $100 and the young cattle, pay the taxes, have Ligon attend to the bank note due 11 September; account of cancer treatment ("A large lump came out through my cheek"; fear of "more Metastases"; radium); clothing by parcel post; travel advice; "you are all I have that is best to live for"; Allie leaving to run a dormitory; staying with Dr Bird.

**A-25. Letter, Papa to Dear Child, "Thursday 6" [6 September 1923], five pages (04-056 to 04-060), with envelope postmarked Union City, Ca., 12 September [year faint] (04-064).** Zella's ink letter; Anna's visit; Waxahachie trip; no beef to be put up; Bruce may want the car; Ira's condition; Ernest's cotton; helping the Dr. make cider; Union City fruit prices; Dr. has five patients.

**A-26. Letter continuation, "Friday, 9. A. M.", unsigned (04-061 to 04-063).** The last plug removed; the hole in his cheek; lump under the chin "like a small butter bean"; light chores for Mrs Henderson; Mr. Ridley and Mrs. Johnson.

**A-27. Letter, Papa to My Dear Child, "Wednesday 11-23-" [day of the month; month not written; internal evidence places it in September 1923], six pages (04-065 to 04-070).** Healing; two meals a day; instructions to ceil, paper or paint the old house at about fifty dollars; Ernest and the scaffolding; chores; labour scarce (with a racial slur retained as written); Allie's departure; Dr. has six patients; Mrs. Johnson.

**A-28. Letter, Papa to My Dear Child, 30 August 1923 [1923-08-30], six pages (04-072 to 04-077), with envelope postmarked Union City, 30 August 1923 (04-071).** Most of the cancer came out; the Ford, the cattle, the note at the Guaranty Trust bank of Waxahachie due 11 September; the tax receipt for Farrar and Kemble, attorneys, in the pending "[[Funds?]] tax business"; Buord Farrar; estimate of the cotton crop by Ernest and Van; a trip would cost $100; Stoker's charge; Allie going to Gunter with cancer; rain, corn, cotton and boll weevil; Duckworth made business manager of "the A.W."

**A-29. Letter, Henry Douglas at Palestine to "Dear Father" (N. L. Douglas), 8 September 1851 [1851-09-08], with two later slips (04-078 to 04-081).** Writing to sister Rebecca; sickness in Anderson County; carpentry work at $1.50 a day; the Christian Church at Palestine to build a house 56 by 34 feet; drought; Mr Mallard bound for old Rutherford; joy at Susannah's [obedience to] the Truth; hope to visit by Christmas.

**A-30. Letter, Ellen Douglas to "Dear Sister Lizzie", 12 January 1862 [1862-01-12], with slip (04-082 to 04-085).** Christmas in her cabin; the young men off to the war; her chills; the children [[Gaskin?]], Angus, Maggie; Beck expecting about 5–11 February and Warren going to the war after her confinement; Sam's cabin; Sue's house begun; Amanda Berry and Lizzie West; love to Father and Mother, Sue and the boys.

**A-31. Notes, H. Douglas at Buchanan to Dear Father, 15 January 1862 [1862-01-15], three blocks on one sheet (04-086).** Went to Brushy to secure "the Land" but failed to get the certificate; Warren will join the army soon; Warren likely to get off in February; fence, wheat, wool to Shreveport netted 26 cents.

**A-32. Letter, Henry at Red Oak to "Dear Sister Lizzie", 6 September 1892 [1892-09-06], with slip "(possibly while in California)" (04-087 to 04-091).** Reproof of the irreligion in California; the meeting at Liberty by Bros Cole & Graves; Clarence and Kate obeyed the gospel, to be 14 on the 8th; others added; crops and rents; Henry Allen to Hood County; Jodie to Erath County; Billy Davis's fall near Palmer; love to Tom & Laura and John & Ann; Sue to teach in Brushy; Mr & Mrs Barton.

**A-33. Letter, Henry Douglas at Palestine to "Dear Mother" (Mrs Mary Douglas, Red Oak), 3 December 1853 [1853-12-03] (slip says 30 December), with address panel (04-092 to 04-096).** Expenses delay his coming; met David Gaslin at Bonner's ferry on the Trinity; Jepse and Warren gone to the Brazos; the Palestine co-operation meeting, about $450 subscribed for evangelizing, Bro. Kendrick preaching, baptisms of children including Addison son of Bro Clark and Albert son of Bro. K; will be home about Christmas.

### Source 05 — Folder 2, correspondence and legal papers (158 pages)

**A-34. Letter, Henry Douglas at Palestine to "Dear Father" (N. L. Douglas), 12 January 1855 [1855-01-12], with slip (05-002 to 05-004).** Partnership with Dr Kendrick for one-third of the practice; opposition from numbers and prejudice; Old Mr Ransom; a cancer case written of to Warren; a visit promised in April.

**A-35. Letter, Henry Douglas at Palestine to "Dear Mother", 8 April 1853 [1853-04-08], with slip (05-005 to 05-007).** Uncle Sam's court engagements and Jepie's and Warren's absences; Susannah to come down with him; the memory of parting from Father near the Willow pond and walking to Porters Bluff; drought and frost; postscript to [[Un.?]] & aunt Cynthia.

**A-36. Letter, H. Douglas in Mississippi County, Missouri, to "Dear Father", 23 June 1860 [1860-06-23], with slip "re driving herd of sheep to Texas" (05-008 to 05-011).** 750 sheep bought in Kentucky with $1,500 including $500 sent by Willis Helm; ferriage at the Iron Banks; disease and losses; four hired hands at $15 a month; route by Fort Gibson ferry and Springfield, Mo.; "win or lose I am bound to go through".

**A-37. Promissory note, Samuel Wallace to N L Douglas, five barrels corn or four dollars, 2 October 1832 [1832-10-02], with slip (05-012).**

**A-38. Letter, James Bass at Murfreesboro, Tenn., to "My Dear Sir" (addressed Nathaniel Douglas, Red Oak Post Office), 18 April 1854 [1854-04-18], with slip (05-013 to 05-015).** Asks for accounts of "our business" after two or three years' silence; has bought from Gen. A. Sublett the balance of the Littleton White tract (about 3,250 acres); wants the taxes checked and information on the Louis Dickson tract on the Trinity at the mouth of five mile creek; expects to be in Texas in the fall.

**A-39. Promissory note, [[S. A.?]] Farrar and F. L. Farrar to G. A. Sublett, $520, 12 October 1852 [1852-10-12], with endorsements: "Pay the within to Mr [[Ledbetter?]]" 28 October 1852; receipt of $15 by N L Douglas for G. A. Sublett 20 April 1853; and slip (05-016 to 05-018).**

**A-40. Tax redemption certificate, W. B. McCabe, assessor and collector of Navarro County, to N L Douglas as agent for G A Sublett, 23 January 1851 [1851-01-23], for 1,280 acres on Subletts Creek listed to A J Porter, sold for the 1848 taxes on 4 February 1850 to C M Winkler; Winkler's release 31 January 1851; docket "Rit of Revocation … Tax and cost $14.48"; slip (05-019 to 05-021).**

**A-41. Due bill, [[S W?]] Webber to N. L. Douglas, five dollars, 21 January 1829 [1829-01-21], with slip "L. W. Webber" (05-022).** The earliest dated document in the supplied scans.

**A-42. Power of attorney, Geo A Sublett of Rutherford County, Tennessee, to "my friend N L Douglas of Ellis County", 28 October 1852 [1852-10-28], to sell his lands in Dallas, Ellis and Navarro counties, convey 640 acres to Mr. Farro and 540 acres to Ephraim Andrews & Co.; acknowledged 29 October 1852 before Benj F Hawkins, Clerk of the County Court of Ellis County, recorded 8 November 1852 in Deed Record A page 163; docket and slip (05-023 to 05-026).**

**A-43. Typed poem, "There is a lonliness of heart", eight lines, "1867 G. Douglas" (05-027).** A typescript, not a manuscript.

**A-44. Deed, Robert Crow of Leon County to G A Sublett, 1,280 acres by Jasper County land certificate 2d Class No 246 of 10 January 1840, for $160, 19 May 1849 [1849-05-19]; acknowledged 22 May 1849 before David M. Brown, Chief Justice; filed 8 August 1849 and recorded 14 December 1849 in Navarro County Book A pages 433–34 by R [[H?]] White; slip "(N. L. Douglas acting as attorney)" (05-028 to 05-031).**

**A-45. Tax redemption certificate, W B McCabe to N L Douglas as agent for G A Sublett, 3 February 1851 [1851-02-03], for 640 acres granted to Robert Crow, sold 25 July 1850 for the 1849 taxes, total $3.08; acknowledged and recorded at Corsicana the same day, Book C page 181; slip dated 1 February (05-032 to 05-034).**

**A-46. Letter, Sadie (Sarah Gail Douglas Headrick) at Bardsdale, California, to "My Dear Aunt" (Lizzie Douglas), 29 September 1893 [1893-09-29], ten leaves in uncertain order, with slip and envelope "from Sadie … Miss Lizzie Douglas, Ferris, Ellis Co." postmarked Bardsdale Aug 9 [[1894?]] (05-035 to 05-045).** Health, housework, furniture, the store; Bruce and Elmo's sayings; Mr. Headrick's back; dreams of the old homestead, "you & Grandma", and Uncle [[George?]]; the *Advocate*; Uncle Geo.'s camp-meeting notice; Bro. Polly; refusal of life insurance on religious grounds; Mrs. Timmons's nervous prostration; photographs.

**A-47. Letter, Sadie at Bardsdale to "Dearest Aunt", 8 August [year not given], signed S. G. H., with slip (05-046 to 05-048).** Grandma Humphrey's operation; Drs. [[Hinckley?]], Bard and Mott; the picnic at Mr. Grimes's; Mr. Wigmore's cart; Mrs. Pickering; the Chadseys; a runaway marriage at Santa Paula; Elmo's and Bruce's remarks; $4.50 earned pitting fruit.

**A-48. Incomplete letter, unsigned, probably Sadie (per slip), four leaves out of order, with envelope to Mrs. Sara Headrick, Red Oak, postmarked [[SANTA BARBARA CAL.?]] 27 April 1921 [1921-04-27], postage due 2 cents (05-049 to 05-053).** Ellen; Mrs. Weatherford and Cousin Sarah Jeter's son; the Clapp ranch sold for $1,500; the church organ; Mr. Pickering's Bible-study proposal; a Holiness sermon; dressmaking for Mrs. [[Timmons?]] and Hattie; Mrs. [[Wigart?]] on baptism. (The 1921 envelope and the letter's contents, which match the 1890s Bardsdale letters, may not belong together.)

**A-49. Fragment of a letter from Fannie, manuscript pages 3–4, undated, with slip "Mentions Lizzie & Laura" (05-054 to 05-056).** Severely darkened; a schoolmate "that left Johnson Co."; a letter to [[Evan?]]; love to Laura.

**A-50. Manuscript of the song "Just before the battle Mother", three verses and chorus, "Written by request Dec 9th /66 For Cousin George, Mollie" [1866-12-09], with household notes at the foot; envelope to Miss Lizzie Douglas, Redoak, postmarked Woodbury, Ten., Apr 10; slips "Poem to Miss Lizzie Douglas From Mollie Woodbury, Tenn." and "Poems & Esays" (05-057 to 05-059).**

**A-51. Fragment of a memoir of George Douglas by his child ("My father"), two leaves out of order, with slip "Concerning Uncle George Douglas – his work at Sylvanna Church – his death Sept. 23, 1923" (05-060 to 05-062).** George laboured with the Church at Sylvana until his death on 23 September 1923; opposed the Sunday School but did not withdraw; died in Georgia and was brought back and buried from the church; after his death the congregation dissolved.

**A-52. Manuscript poem, untitled ("There in mist my vision failing"), four stanzas (05-064 to 05-065), and poem "In answer to the Author of 'Georgie'" (05-066 to 05-067), with slip (05-063).** Unsigned, undated verse; "Platonic friendship at my years".

**A-53. Song ballad "Swinging in the lane", docketed Jan the 22, 1881 [1881-01-22], with slip (05-068 to 05-070).** Rosa Nell and Aunt Jemima Brown.

**A-54. Three verse fragments on nature and talents (05-071 to 05-072).** Faded scraps: "the hills of the [[West?]]", "The beauties of nature", "our tallents were not improved".

**A-55. Receipt, A. Trippet & Son, Waxahachie, cotton bought, Oct [[14?]] 1874 [1874-10-14], cash to balance $[[26.75?]]; slip attributes the sale to Geo. Douglas (05-073).**

**A-56. Duplicate scans of the 1888 endorsement (05-074 to 05-076) and of the 1883 sermon notes (05-077 to 05-079).** See A-02 and A-03.

**A-57. Essay, "The beauty of female culture", signed Sarah E. Douglas, April [[21?]] [year not given], with slip "The Beauty of Culture by Sarah Douglas" (05-080 to 05-081).**

**A-58. Damaged essay or verse on friendship, unsigned (05-082).** Largely unread.

**A-59. Composition, "Parting From Friends" by Emma Bell, marked "Very good", with docket and a short added reflection (05-083 to 05-085).**

**A-60. Letter, B. F. Lillard at Murfreesboro, Tenn., to Mrs. Douglas and Mrs. Orr, Ferris, 6 December 1883 [1883-12-06], "Per Ida", with envelope postmarked Ferris 8 December 1883 and slip (05-086 to 05-088).** Chancellor Burton died in term; a Circuit Court judge's decree dividing the Alexander land between Jacob Wright's nephews and nieces and the heirs of Mary Wright; Lillard's appeal to the Supreme Court.

**A-61. Unsigned draft power of attorney, Mrs Mary Douglas of Ellis County to B. F. Lillard, 1883, with slip (05-089 to 05-090).**

**A-62. Letter, M. M. Dill at Murfreesboro to Mr Geo. Douglas, Ferris, 26 February 1883 [1883-02-26], with slip (05-091 to 05-093).** No other attorney needed; Cousin Docia Bivins, Isaac N. Wright, Mrs Nance, Mandy; all heirs to join as complainants; the 640-acre tract, the River Tract and the Arkansas lands.

**A-63. Letter, B. F. Lillard to Mrs Mary Douglas, 30 December 1882 [1882-12-30] (address corrected from Ennis to Ferris), with addition 11 February 1883, postscript and slip (05-094 to 05-096).** Death of "your Cousin Mrs Alexander daughter of your uncle Jacob Wright"; the first cousins who inherit (3 Goodloe, 3 Dill, 2 Brasher, 6 Thompson Wright); 680 acres assessed at $8,500, 300 acres in Arkansas; the 640 acres sold for $6,400 on credit; the defective conveyance; the statute of limitations.

**A-64. Letter, B. F. Lillard to Mr Geo. Douglas, Ferris, 22 March 1883 [1883-03-22], seven pages with postscript (05-097 to 05-104; scan 101 repeats 99).** Long defence of Lillard's character and standing against George's letter of 15 March; Judge Peter Turney, Judge Burton, John Woods, Col. N. D. Robinson, Cumberland University; whisky prescribed for rheumatism from army wounds; Alexander's manoeuvres; Albert Jetton's store.

**A-65. Envelope and slip, Lewis Jetton to Mrs Mary Douglas or Cynthia Orr, Redoake, postmarked [[illegible]]VILLE, Tenn., Feb 14 [1883] (05-105; repeated 05-109).**

**A-66. Letter, B. F. Lillard to Mr Geo Douglas, Ferris, 26 February 1883 [1883-02-26], with envelope and slip (05-106 to 05-108).** The Travis deed of the 640 acres for $6,400; A. M. Alexander's acknowledgment 15 July 1881; the privy examination by D. M. McKnight, his half-brother; registration September 1882; Rebecca Alexander's death 11 November 1882; the fee question; margin note about the demand for the land.

**A-67. Note, Albert Jetton at Murfreesboro to "Dear Aunt", 12 February 1883 [1883-02-12], recommending Lillard (05-110).**

**A-68. Letter, L Jetton to "My Sisters [[Cinty?]] & Mary", 12 February 1883 [1883-02-12], warning against Lillard ("too fast", "working for big fees"), to be kept "in secrecy in the family"; postscript "I believe A. M. A will do right" (05-111 to 05-113).**

**A-69. Letter, B. F. Lillard to Mrs. Mary Douglas, "written 15th – no year date" per slip [after 14 February 1883], beginning with a postscript; with envelope to Ferris (05-114 to 05-116).** J. N. Spence's report that Alexander will give up the lands; the 640-acre deed voidable; Rebecca's deathbed promise to her father; some heirs holding back for business reasons.

**A-70. Note, A Jetton at Murfreesboro to "Aunt", 14 February 1883 [1883-02-14]: does not advise a suit, only recommends Lillard; slip (05-117).**

**A-71. Letter, L A Jetton at Readyville, Tenn., to Mrs Sue Bell, "My very Dear Cousin", 20 February 1870 [1870-02-20], with slip (05-118 to 05-121).** Illness; Aunt Clark and the Legislature; Sallie Wieden's three [[Divorces?]]; John's new boy; death of Old Aunt Polly Wright two weeks earlier; the marriages of "the girls" (Sarah to Tullison, Smith to Popley, Marier to [[Darnum?]] [[Vaught?]], etc.); Mr Akers on sons-in-law; Pap's poverty; love to Ellen.

**A-72. Letter, Jesse Douglas at Cross Creek, Travis County, to George & Lizzie, 30 August 1869 [1869-08-30], continued 15 September, with slip "Jessie Douglas, Cross Creeks, Tex." (05-122 to 05-125).** Flood, sickness, rent-hunting near Austin, thoughts of Arkansas stopped by his mother-in-law; birth of Earl Stewart Douglas on 2 August 1869, ten pounds; crops; "tell Sarah I am coming to see her".

**A-73. Letter, Jesse Douglas at Alexander, Texas, to "Dear Daughter" (Sarah, "Sal"), Sunday 15 June 1884 [1884-06-15], with slip (05-126 to 05-128).** A school of 18–20 scholars under the Hon J C Roe beginning 7 July; barbecues at Alexander and Carlton.

**A-74. Letter, Jesse Douglas at Cross Creek, Travis Co., to George, 28 November 1869 [1869-11-28], torn edge, with slip (05-129 to 05-133).** Sickness (Nancy, Eli, Earl); one bale of cotton, 434 lb, paid the debts and left one dollar; cannot move while old Mrs Stewart lives; George to make the figures on "the Old Farm"; 320 acres on Kickapoo Creek, Hood County (the G C Briscoe headright), bought for Sarah at the cost of his horse Billy Capshaw; Eli weighs 52 lb; directed to Henry, "most Central"; address Drip Springs; election at hand.

**A-75. Warranty deed, the heirs at law of Mary Douglas to Lizzie Douglas, thirty acres of the A. J. Porter survey, in consideration of services to Mary in her last years, 8 October 1886 [1886-10-08] (05-135 to 05-136, scanned in reverse order), signed George Douglas, Warren Douglas, Laura Robison, Geo. Stanford, Ellen Stanford, R. C. Farrar, M. D. Farrar, W. E. Bell, Johnnie Bell, H. Headrick, Sarah G. Headrick, [[Thomas J. Robison?]], H Douglas; acknowledgments by George (Ellis Co., 9 October 1886), Warren (Johnson Co., 27 January 1887), Thomas J and Laura Robison (Ventura Co., California, 14 October 1893), and H Douglas, the Stanfords, Farrars, Bells and Headricks (Ellis Co., 15 April 1891); filed 28 May 1896 and recorded 29 May 1896 in Book 92 page 576 by T F Thompson; slips (05-134, 05-137, 05-152, 05-154 to 05-155).**

**A-76. Warranty deed on a printed form, the heirs at law of Mary Douglas (Nancy Douglas, Earl Douglas, J A McDaniel, Emily J. McDaniel, W. D. (Davis) Duff, Ora J Duff, E L Winiford) to Lizzie Douglas, the same thirty acres, for one dollar and services, 4 June 1891 [1891-06-04]; acknowledged in Erath County (Nancy and Earl, 4 June 1891), Ellis County (McDaniels and Duffs, 5 July 1892) and Oklahoma Territory (E L Winiford, 25 March 1893); filed and recorded 4 January 1894 in Book 46 page 358 by J. R. Champion; with envelope from Oklahoma postmarked 25 March 1893 and slip (05-138 to 05-142).**

**A-77. Deed, the heirs of Lucy I Winniford (E L Winniford and wife Millie of Oklahoma Territory; Haller/Walter Roper and wife Mary H Roper and H. D. Winniford of Navarro County; John McCrary and wife Alice E McCrary of Floyd County) to Lizzie Douglas, for $160, sixteen acres being an undivided one-fifth of the 80-acre Winniford survey in the A. J. Porter 1280, which came to Lucy "by deed from Mary Douglas jointly with us as heirs of Rebecca Winniford", 13 October 1890 [1890-10-13]; acknowledgments in Navarro (2 January 1891), Floyd (4 March 1891) and Oklahoma (4 April 1891) counties; filed 5 May 1891 by E P Hawkins, recorded Book 62 page 590; slip (05-143 to 05-149).**

**A-78. Inventory of the estate of N. L. Douglas, deceased, given by Mary Douglas, relict, sworn 5 October 1874 [1874-10-05] before Wm Hancock, J.P. Precinct No 3; appraisers E. A. Norman and W R Duff; and a second copy stating the Porter patent date 18 June 1849 (05-150 to 05-151, 05-153).** 640 acres of the A. J. Porter 1280 on Subletts creek (94 improved at $15, 546 unimproved at $5), 9 horses, 20 cattle, 14 hogs, 20 bee hives, trace chains, plow, log chain, grubbing hoe, part set of wagon irons, grindstone.

**A-79. Deed, Mary Douglas to Lizzie Douglas, 82 acres more or less of the Porter survey on Subletts creek "about 10 miles N. E. from the town of Waxahachie", in consideration of Lizzie's quitclaim of the N L Douglas estate, Mary retaining use for life, 25 February 1875 [1875-02-25]; acknowledged at Palmer before Wm Hancock J P; filed 21 August 1875, Book O page 3 (docket) / pages 344 (certificate); slip (05-156 to 05-158, scanned in reverse order).**

### Source 06 — Folder 3, correspondence and legal papers (118 pages)

**A-80. Deed, George Douglas to Lizzie Douglas, 1 April 1887 [1887-04-01], for $700 his undivided 60 acres of the 80-acre Winniford tract and for $300 twenty acres of the Porter survey; acknowledged 2 April 1887 before R. P. Mackay; recorded 9 April 1887 in Vol 47 pages 228–9 by B. F. Hawkins, deputy R. M. Beale; slip (06-002 to 06-006).**

**A-81. Warranty deed on a printed form, H D Winniford, Walter Roper, Mary F Roper, John McCrary and A. E. McCrary, as heirs at law of Mary Douglas, to Lizzie Douglas, the thirty acres, signed at Big Springs, Texas, 22 July 1891 [1891-07-22]; acknowledged in Howard County (22 July 1891) and Floyd County (10 November 1891); filed 28 May 1896 and recorded 29 May 1896, Vol 92 page 574, by T. F. Thompson; docket and slip (06-007 to 06-011).**

**A-82. Letter, L A Jetton at Readyville to Miss Lizzie Douglas, "My Dearest Cousin", 20 March 1883 [1883-03-20], with envelope postmarked Readyville 23 March and slip (06-012 to 06-015).** Family news; loneliness "only a little negro with me"; how Cousin Rebecca Alexander caught cold watching the comet and died; Cousin Andy says her will was stolen from her drawer two weeks before her death; the Jacob Wright estate said to be worth about $[[26000?]].

**A-83. Letter, L A Jetton at Readyville to Miss Lizzie Douglas, 3 March 1878 [1878-03-03], with slip "(mentions death[?] of Susanna Douglas bell)" (06-016 to 06-018).** Whooping cough; a wedding and reception at Cousin Miriam's; Jimmie and Mary; sympathy: "I loved Cousin Sue on earth I hope to meet her in heaven".

**A-84. Letter, [[Maybey?]] (Maybel/Marybee) Jetton at Readyville to Miss Lizzie Douglas, "Dear Cousin", 8 April 1874 [1874-04-08], with slip (06-019 to 06-022).** Readyville kin; new hope Church; Ma and uncle Albert teach Sunday School; a scrapbook: "tell Cousin George to Send me some pretty verses"; longing for "my old home".

**A-85. Letter, L A Jetton at Readyville to Miss Lizzie Douglas, 1 February 1874 [1874-02-01], with slip "(Lewis ?)" (06-023 to 06-025).** School at Readyville; Bro Rice Sewell at newhope; Mr Barr and Aunt Jane; Marybee's picture; thanks for money from "your Ma selling the saddle"; Charlie's wife; Eddie Jolly.

**A-86. Letter, Fannie (per slip, Columbia Goodloe) at Russellville, Ala., to "My Dear Cousin" (Lizzie), 21 June 1874 [1874-06-21], with slip (06-026 to 06-029).** Keeping house for Uncle Dr Clarke; Uncle John; Brother French and his boys Russell and Eugene; Broth Tommie and "little N L"; Broth Bobbie; Sis Mary; "I am glad Pa is so much pleased with Texas".

**A-87. Letters of Lizzie Graves in Alabama to Lizzie Douglas (06-030 to 06-058), seven items:** (a) undated fragment, reading order 32 then 31, about sewing, Bud Joe and Brazil, crops, Valentines and "Flora" (06-030 to 06-032); (b) "Saturday 19th", Grand ma sick, Aunt Jane's family coming, the new saddle and pony, "Pas eyes are getting worse" (06-033 to 06-035); (c) Pratville, Ala., 10 March 1867 [1867-03-10], first letter from the new home Mosshead or Buttonwood Hall, "I would much rather live in Texas" (06-036 to 06-038, continuing on 32 and 31); (d) Mosshead, 15 September 1868 [1868-09-15] (slip 16th), three corpses at one sight, her Sunday School class (06-039 to 06-043); (e) Mosshead, 30 September 1867 [1867-09-30], chills, fruit, the Methodist kin, Frank reading the Bible, an evening ride with "my beau" (06-044 to 06-048); (f) Mosshead, 29 August 1867 [1867-08-29], sickness, "an Ala. girl", the search for a Christian church, "Pa and I are alone" (06-049 to 06-051); (g) Mosshead, 10 May 1867 [1867-05-10], Pa's eyes, red pepper for chills, the Confederate and Federal grave decorations at Tusk[egee?] and Montgomery, the Methodist reunion, corn at $2 (06-052 to 06-054); (h) Mosshead, 20 November 1868 [1868-11-20], Uncle J. back from California, earthquakes, Greenville, beaus, "I have not heard of Mr Winnifords marriage" (06-055 to 06-058).

**A-88. Letter, M E Goodloe at Randolph to "Miss Lizzie D., Dear Niece", 12 February 1865 [1865-02-12], with slip "(Niece) M E Goodloe, Randolph – (Field)" (06-059 to 06-061).** Henry Orr's visit on the way to his command; a party at Uncle Tom's; "my brittest hopes have been blasted"; Granvill and Babe; a letter from George Douglas; sweethearts assigned to Lizzie and Laura; Nannie; Mary Orr; Sue.

**A-89. Letter fragment, Laura and Lizzie to George, "Friday Sept 18" and "Saturday evening 20th", year not given [context: September 1863], with slip "(Civil War) (no year date)" (06-062 to 06-064).** The murder of Mrs [[Stinnet?]] by her daughter Mollie (the Claunch case); Sister's children; a twelve-dollar saddle; heavily darkened.

**A-90. Letter fragment, probably Lizzie to George (per slip), undated [after April 1863], reading order 67 then 66 (06-065 to 06-067).** Joda and Mr Banks in Dallas; things sent, bridle, blanket; wheat, the tithe tax, speculators and "old Magruder"; a sack of flour and fat bacon for the men at Sabine.

**A-91. Letter, Lizzie at Brushy to Dear George, 21–22 April 1865 [1865-04-21], with slip (06-068 to 06-072).** Joda's arrival on the 18th; George back from Velasco; Dock; thread and pins; the flour speculation; a side saddle for Laura; Laura's school of 16–19 scholars; Cousin Mollie and Mrs. Bishop; the hanging of the Stiles boys and "this mob law"; Fayette and Bob; Henry and Ellen gone home; the well; Methodist Jones at Liberty; Joda plowing; letters from [[Fran?]]; scissors; "best love to Warren and yourself".

**A-92. Letter fragment, Lizzie to George, manuscript "Page 5th" and following, with postscript 20 March, year not given (06-073 to 06-075).** George's rude word to Sis; recommending correspondents; the nag bought from Mr Rutherford; Jeppe bought Laura's pony; the well; advice on George's handwriting, spelling and paper; Joe home, "taken prisner and Paroled by the Feds".

**A-93. Fragment, Laura to George, "Thursday 28" [Apr.], both sides (06-076 to 06-078).** Mr. Graves planting corn; the boy whose hand was mashed off.

**A-94. Fragment, Lizzie to George, "17th", with slip "Tells of battle below Shreveport with Reb capture of 2000 prisoners" (06-079 to 06-081).** Very faint; south wind; Sunday school begins; "all well on Brushy".

**A-95. Note, [Laura per slip] to Dear George, "By the fire Sandy [[illegible]] Feb 1st 1865" [1865-02-01] (the family edition reads 1863 and a signature "Hat"), with slip "Feb. 11, 1865(?)" (06-082 to 06-084).** A run on Blazes leading Jenney; spinning; "Lizzie & Jeannie have entire deserted us".

**A-96. Letter No. 8, Lizzie at Brushy to Dear brother (George), Thursday night 14 April 1864 [1864-04-14], continued the 15th (06-085 to 06-087).** Faded: the Dr.; all well on Hack; Jeppe; Beck and [[Wash?]] from Grape Vine Springs; Ellen wanting to move; militia and deserters; Mrs White's six-shooter; turkeys, pet deer, a panther; Joda's trip; the peace question and the cotton trade; Genl McCullough's trap; Rome and the Eagle Pass teams.

**A-97. Letter, Lizzie at Brushy to Dear George, 13–16 November 1864 [1864-11-13], with slip (06-088 to 06-092).** Mrs Justin's funeral sermon at Liberty; Aunt Bettie's move to Arkansas; a sow and shoats for ten dollars specie; the well; Lincoln's election and four more years; [[Childsy?]] and Mr Grounds from camp; Henry and Ellen home; Warren, Beck and "Griffin"; Dock [[Wyrman?]] of Co. G, Bates' Reg at Velasco; Ed Dickson guarding prisoners at Tyler; Rome and San Antonio; Jeppe's wool; the Marshall family from Arkansas; [[Lidda?]] Baker's gold nose; Jane Green's death.

**A-98. Letter No. 4, Lizzie at Brushy to Brother George, 19–20 February 1864 [1864-02-19], with slip (06-093 to 06-095).** Letters from Fayette and Jim; Jim's wound at Chickamauga; Tole Hamilton killed at Ringgold; the Goodloe deserters and Squire; Will safe in the North; Joda on George's battalion; Gurley's regiment; Quantrill's men and [[Sherrol?]]; militia armed with sticks; Emmie, Cousin Mollie, Laura's request for George's verses, baby May.

**A-99. Letter No. 13 (slip: 18), Lizzie at Brushy to Dear Brother, 28 June 1864 [1864-06-28], with slip (06-096 to 06-099).** Death of Uncle Alfred on 5 June, eighteen days after his wound; Aunt Bettie's collapse; his last words; burial between John Morgan and Docter Gardner; Bob [[Head?]]; cousin Gran; Parsons' men to Houston; the Methodist meeting at Liberty; marriages of old people, the widow Smith's hundred negroes; Fayette's letters and George's 24-page letter; the baby named Sarah by Gale; the Colony Presbyterian meeting; John Andrews and Adolphus.

**A-100. Letter No. 16, Sue (Bell) at Liberty school house and Lizzie at home, to Dear Brother (George), 18/19–21 August 1864 [1864-08-19], with slip (06-100 to 06-104).** Paper by Mr. Rockett; Rome hauling cotton to Laredo; the old Liberty Spring; fleas "as thick as flies down at Sabine"; a spelling match; Warren Payne dying at aunt Catherine's; Lynn Norman, Eppa's regiment to Arizona, Gran Jetton's well, the Chappells; Tom Witherspoon's head wound; George's furlough number 13 and Walker in command; Lt. Allen; Ruth, "sisters black girl"; Sam and the post office.

**A-101. Letter No. 9, Lizzie at Brushy to Brother Geo., 30 April 1864 [1864-04-30], with two slips (06-105 to 06-107).** Jeppe a widower again: Gail died Saturday 23 April; the baby brought home; Bro Jones's sermons and Parson Austin's reply; the Methodist preacher Marshall; Joda's ox train to Milligan.

**A-102. Letter No. 16 (second so numbered), Lizzie at Brushy to Dear Brother, 6–7 September 1864 [1864-09-06], with slip addressed "George Douglas, Galveston" (06-108 to 06-112).** Knife by Mr. Collins; Frances Blair; Jeppe from Hack Creek with 130 bushels of corn; Henry's lost patients; sudden deaths; J. Bell's wound; old Toby sold for $23 specie; Mr. L. Evans from Sabine; "I am glad old Magruder is gone"; the rival Methodist and Christian meetings, Parson Hill's saying, Bro Jones on the unity of the church, four added; Parson Austin's courting; Fayett and Jim captured; Walk Hatter's four months as a prisoner.

**A-103. Letter Number 15, Lizzie at Brushy to Brother Geo., 5–6 August 1864 [1864-08-05], with slip addressed "George Douglas, Sabine Pass" (06-113 to 06-116).** Washing day; George's change in the regiment; P. H.'s marriage; the scarcity of young men; Ed Dickson's candy pulling at uncle Graves's; the county election (Frank Hawkins, Love, Coltham, [[Norvell?]]); the militia and a fifty-year-old's threatened suit; Em Gibbs; Bro Goodman's discourse; largely faded closing pages.

**A-104. Two torn fragments of a letter signed "Sister Lizzie" to George, undated [spring 1864] (06-117 to 06-118).** Rumours of whipping Steele in Arkansas and capturing two hundred wagons; seven men hung near Kentucky Town; teamsters keeping out of the army ("got to drive his waggons! getting biger pay than any of us").

| 51 | event | Mrs Claunch murder case | Murder of Mrs Claunch by her daughter under consideration in the Waxahachie court | Sept 1863 | 1863-09 | 04-017-P03 | I have visited Waxahachie twice — a very important case being under Consideration in the Court — To wit; The murder of a Mrs Claunch by her Daughter — The case has not yet been decided but from the evidence I have heard believe she will come clear — but not in the Eyes of the people — as innocent of the offence. |
| 52 | church | Red Oak Camp Ground | Camp meeting at Red Oak Camp Ground, Sept 1863, smaller than former years; 'Shouting times' | Sept 1863 | 1863-09 | 04-017-P03; 04-017-P04 | From Waxa on Monday I went to Red Oak Camp Ground — Camp meeting being on hand ... They had Shouting times that night |
| 53 | event | Henry's command | June 1863 ordered west; 1 Aug to Mississippi Valley near Natchez; 31 Aug brigade started for Arkansas; 'Seen the Elephant in La'; Genl Steele; Majors' Div; Walker's | Aug 1863 | 1863-08 | 04-018-P01 | About The 1st of Aug. We moved to the Miss. Valley near Natchez — remained there a Couple of Weeks & then were ordered to Our former field (Ark). On the 31st of Aug the Brigade Started on their way rejoicing. We had Seen the Elephant in La |
| 54 | event | Henry's squad | Surprised 28 Aug 1863 at 3 AM; 30 horses lost; Capt [[Vardeman?]] | 28 of Aug 1863 | 1863-08-28 | 04-018-P02 | Whilst Scouting on the Miss Our Squad turned over about 30 fine Horses their arms &c to the Yankee ... They were Completely Surprised at 3 oclk AM on the 28 of Aug |
| 55 | person | Bob; Jeffie; Lizzie; John; Laura; Mother; Sam Austin | Persons in the Sept 1863 letter | Sept 1863 | 1863-09 | 04-018-P02; 04-018-P03 | Bob got to Camps the day before I left ... Jeffie was down last week. / We Learn Lizzie spent Sunday with you & she Returned to John on Monday. [[Laura?]] Spent last night here. Mother has had a very Sick Spell is getting over it. ... [[Br?]] Sam Austin Called in today Returning from the meeting |
| 56 | event | Lafayette and James | Heavy fight 22 July 1863; regiment went in with 320, came out with about 80; James narrow escape | 22nd of July 1863 | 1863-07-22 | 04-018-P03 | We recently Recd a Letter from Lafayette of [[date?]] 1st of Aug. Their Brigade had a heavy fight on the 22nd of July — their Regt went into Battle with 320 & Came out with about 80 — James who was in the [[illegible]] had a narrow Escape |
| 57 | person | Hage; Clem | Margin: their company surrendered while stacking arms | 1863 | 1863 | 04-018-P04 | Those of their Co that was not killed had to Surrender & while They were Stacking arms Hage [[illegible]] through [[illegible]] & Clem [[illegible]] was found at hind |
| 58 | event | Bob (R D G) | Old hunting companion and schoolmate writes from Cold Springs; hunted gobblers on old Brushy; nearly four years since he saw George; weighs 175; Co. F | Sept 19th /63 | 1863-09-19 | 04-019-P01; 04-019-P04 | Cold Springs Texas / Sept 19th /63 ... it has been A long time since we used to meet down on old Brushy and give the old gobblers a round ... nearly Four years have pass Since I Saw you ... I weigh 175 pounds and the largest Devil in co. (F.) |
| 59 | event | Bob (R D G) | Big meeting in camp, forty mourners | Sept 1863 | 1863-09 | 04-019-P05 | There is a big meeting going on in camp. There was about forty mourners last night. They haven't got me yet |
| 60 | person | Bob (R D G) | Brothers east of the Mississippi; signs R D G / Bob | Sept 1863 | 1863-09 | 04-020-P01; 04-020-P03; 04-020-P05 | I have not heard from my Brothers East of the miss for Two or three months ... As Ever Your Cousin / R D G ... Bob |
| 61 | event | James (Tupelo) | Wounded 22 July [1864] charging the enemy's works; left wrist and hand; fever from mid-August; 60 days furlough in October to Mobile; stopped at Demopolis with Mrs Brassfield | Jan 25th/65 | 1865-01-25 | 04-021-P01; 04-021-P03 | Tupelo Mississippi Jan 25th/65 / Dear Brother / As Brother Lafayette will Start home in a few days on furlough ... I was painfully wounded on the 22nd of July while Charging the enemys works The ball Struck me on the the left wrist and passed throw my hand |
| 62 | event | James (Tupelo) | Federals destroyed railroad; in hospital at Columbus Miss.; hand still running; wrist stiff; brigade about 400 of five regiments; four of old Co I fit for duty; Lafayette 75 days furlough | Jan 1865 | 1865-01 | 04-022-P02; 04-022-P04; 04-023-P04 | Thair is very few of our Brigade left only about 4 hundred out of five Regiments / Thair is only four of old Co "I" left that is able for duty. one to evry 7 is furloughed to go to Texas Brother Lafayette gets a 75 days furlough to go home |
| 63 | person | Squire; the Jetton boys (Jeppe, Billy, Charly); the Six Texas Boys | Named in the Tupelo letter; Jeppe Jetton in prison | Jan 1865 | 1865-01 | 04-024-P04 | Squire gets a furlough ... I have not heard from our cousins (the Jetton boys) lately Jeppe is in prison Billy & Charly were well when last heard from. The Six Texas Boys were all well when last heard from. |

According to announcement there was dinner on the ground. And such another dinner. About the finest ever spread at Sylvana. Among visitors present was White Stirman and [[deleted: illegible]] wife ... and Nellie Russell ... From Oak Cliff were three carloads of people, Jeff Barclay ... the wife of J. C. Estes, the preacher, Bro. Stevens, mother wife and sister, Bro. Melton and family and Bro. Holsenback, and wife. ... Mrs. Dr. Mitchell and family, and Bro. Dunn's family were out from Waxahachie. |

| 69 | person | Lucile; Larkin; Kate | Lucile ill at Kate's; Larkin's disease malignant; company forbidden |  |  | 04-028-P04; 04-028-P05; 04-029-P04 | No change to report in Lucile's condition ... Larkin is much worse; the disease taking a malignant form day before yesterday ... At Kate's. I found Lucile not so well as last Sunday |
| 70 | event | Ellis County cotton crop | Crop a failure on 11 August (year not given); worms destroyed bolls | the eleventh of August |  | 04-029-P01 | The Ellis Co. cotton crop is a failure today the eleventh of August. The worms have completely destroyed all the bolls and squares, and blooms are scarcer than I ever saw |
| 71 | church | Ila Duncan; Mayfaire; Bros Duncan | Confessions and baptisms at the meeting |  |  | 04-030-P01; 04-030-P02 | Ila Duncan and Mayfaire made the confession and will be baptized this evening — meeting will close tomorrow night. / Came up today and had a real civilized dinner with [[Bros?]] Duncan. |
| 72 | event | Red Oak | Old wooden centre of Red Oak burned |  |  | 04-030-P03 | Jesse Ligon and family have returned to Red Oak, bag and baggage. ... Did I tell you that the old wooden center of Red Oak burned out from brick to brick? Well the old rat traps are gone. Some loss heavily — |
| 73 | person | G. D. (George Douglas) to Zella | Signs 'Affectionately G. D.'; 'My peach preserves were highly praised' |  |  | 04-030-P04; 04-030-P05 | Affectionately G. D. / Do the best you can for yourself while you are there. Dont bother about me. / My peach preserves were highly praised |
| 74 | person | Clarence & Kate; Sid Farrar; Glenn; Roland | Stayed with Clarence & Kate; puncture fixed at Rockett with Sid Farrar; Kate's children look bad; Roland's house in the pasture | Wednesday Eve |  | 04-032-P02; 04-032-P03; 04-033-P01 | I staid with Clarence & Kate Sunday night ... so I drove to Rockett on the rim, and with the help of Sid Farrar soon had it righted up. ... Glenn was not well ... Will likely come to Roland's house in the pasture. |
| 75 | person | Aunt Nannie; Ruth; Elmer Carter; Edwina | Letter from Paris forwarded; Aunt Nannie; Ruth's plum jelly; Elmer Carter takes the cow; Edwina's letter |  |  | 04-033-P02; 04-033-P04 | The letter to you from Paris I opened ... Will send yours to your Aunt Nannie ... Ruth gave me a glass of plum jelly ... Elmer Carter is very glad to get all of the Cow. ... Tell Edwina I enjoyed reading her good letter hugely. |
| 76 | person | Ed | Zella staying where Ed's expenses are enormous | Tuesday Morning |  | 04-034-P03 | I know Ed's expenses must be enormous; but be sure you do not add any to them. |
| 77 | event | G. D.'s housekeepers | Housekeepers left for Bartlett, Texas; George 'baching' |  |  | 04-034-P03; 04-034-P04 | Our house keepers packed up their goods Saturday, and Sunday Evening loaded them on two wagons, and Monday morning pulled out for Bartlett Texas. ... So my prospects for baching grows brighter from day to day. ... My nieces cant understand why I persist in staying at home |
| 78 | event | Feterita patch | Hens water below the feterita patch |  |  | 04-035-P01 | They mainly water just below the Feterita patch at a swamp spring, and the feterita furnishes them fine picking. |

| 98 | event | Papa (George Douglas) to Zella | Letter from Union City (California postmark) about cancer treatment; wants $100 for the Ford; sell young cattle; note at Waxahachie bank due 11 Sept | Sept 3 — 1923 | 1923-09-03 | 04-048-P01; 04-048-P04; 04-048-P05; 04-049-P02 | Union City Sept 3 — 1923 / My Dear Child — ... I want a hundred dollars for the Ford. No trade. ... Sell the young cattle as it wont pay to winter them. Put up feed for one cow only. Pay the taxes, and have Ligon to attend to note due the / Waxahachie Bank, due the Eleventh of September. |
| 99 | event | Papa | A large lump came out through his cheek; fears more metastases; radium scattered the trouble; 'This last will be four' | Sept 1923 | 1923-09 | 04-049-P05; 04-050-P02; 04-050-P03; 04-050-P05 | A large lump came out through my cheek, and there is another to follow not so large. ... The thing I fear is that more Metastases may set up — / The radium had scattered the trouble into my cheek ... This last will be four that has been caught up with |
| 100 | money | Papa | Trip would cost $100; wants all debts at home paid | Sept 1923 | 1923-09 | 04-051-P04 | You may have to come out here; and it will take a hundred dollars to cover the trip; and I want all debts at home paid so as to know how best to do. |
| 101 | event | Papa | Cannot come back by Nashville because of the altitude over the Cumberland Mountains; Anna's visit | Sept 1923 | 1923-09 | 04-052-P03 | No! I cant come back by Nashville. I would not risk the altitude over the Cumberland Mountains. I am glad Anna stopped to see you. |
| 102 | event | Papa | 'you are all I have that is best to live for'; car cranking days over | Sept 1923 | 1923-09 | 04-053-P01 | I would love for you to be here, for I get very blue and lonesome at times, and you are all I have that is best to live for. With the number of my years I cant stay here long ... my car cranking days are over. |
| 103 | person | Allie; Dr Bird | Allie leaves to run a dormitory; George staying with Dr Bird | Sept 1923 | 1923-09 | 04-054-P02 | Allie leaves tomorrow for [[illegible]] to run a Dormitory. I am staying with Dr Bird. Papa. |

If Ernest wants to kill one and peddle it out ... If Bruce wants the car very much, he will get rid of his and buy ... Very sorry to hear of Ira's condition. ... Have Ernest and Van to make a careful estimate of the cotton crop in Bales. |

| 107 | event | Papa | Last plug removed; hole in cheek made by the cancer coming out; lump under chin like a small butter bean | Friday, 9. A. M. |  | 04-061-P02; 04-061-P03; 04-062-P02 | The last plug is removed, and future treatments not needed unless more Metastasis sets up. ... It will take a long time to heal the hole in my cheek made by the cancer coming out ... The lump under my chin is gradually getting less, thinner and flatter, about like a small butter bean. |
| 108 | person | Mr. Ridley; Mrs. Johnson | Fellow patients |  |  | 04-063-P02; 04-063-P03 | Mr. Ridley is improving right along ... Mrs. Johnson is getting along very slowly. |
| 109 | event | Papa | House repairs: ceil, paper or paint; about fifty dollars; Ernest to bring lumber for scaffolding | Wednesday 11-23- |  | 04-066-P01; 04-067-P01; 04-068-P01 | There is three ways to stop this. Ceil over the old ceiling with new lumber, paper or paint the walls. ... Oil, paint, and putting it on, will likely cost about fifty dollars |
| 110 | event | Papa | Labor scarce; racial remark retained as documentary text |  |  | 04-069-P01 | White folks wont work [[inserted: or dont work]], and niggers are scarce. I do not know when I have seen a white man hunting work. |
| 111 | place | Zella Douglas | Envelope Union City GA [sic] AUG 30 1923 | AUG 30 1923 | 1923-08-30 | 04-071-P02 | UNION CITY / AUG 30 / 6 PM / GA / 1923 |
| 112 | event | Papa | Aug 30 1923: most of the cancer came out; home maybe middle of October | Aug 30 - 1923 | 1923-08-30 | 04-072-P01 | Most of the cancer came out, a little left, cant tell just how much. ... If no further trouble comes up, and I stay until fully healed, it may be the middle of October |
| 113 | money | Papa; Mr. Ligon; Guaranty Trust bank of Waxahachie | Note due 11 Sept at Guaranty Trust bank; tax receipt to Farrar and Kemble attorneys for the suit in the [[Funds?]] tax business; Buord Farrar attorney | Aug 30 1923 | 1923-08-30 | 04-073-P01; 04-074-P01 | go to Mr. Ligon, get the note on The Guaranty Trust bank of Waxahachie, due the eleventh day of September ... take it Farrar and Kemble attys, and leave with them. They may want / it as evidence in the suit pending in the [[Funds?]] tax business. / Then tell Buord Farrar to check on Red Oak for what funds he may have to have in my case as attorney. |
| 114 | person | Stoker | Made an awful charge overhauling the machine | Aug 1923 | 1923-08 | 04-076-P01 | Stoker made an awful charge. Did he itemize it? He must have overhauled the whole machine. |
| 115 | person | Allie | Going to Gunter to take charge of a dormitory; she has cancer | Aug 1923 | 1923-08 | 04-076-P01 | Allie is making ready to go to Gunter, take charge of a Dormitory, and put the children in school. ... She has cancer |
| 116 | event | Duckworth; the A.W. | Duckworth made business manager of the A.W.; circular calling for help to print it | Aug 1923 | 1923-08 | 04-077-P01 | Duckworth has been made business manager of the A.W. He has put out a circular letter to the brethren, calling for help to print it in Texas saying he has no money and that the paper has none |
| 117 | relationship | Henry Douglas; N. L. Douglas | Identifying slip: letter to Mr. N. L. Douglas from son Henry, Palestine; a second slip: letters by Henry and Ellen Douglas | Sept. 8, 1851 | 1851-09-08 | 04-078-P01; 04-078-P02 | To Mr. N. L. Douglas / from son, / Henry Douglas / Palestine, Texas / Sept. 8, 1851 / Letters written by / Henry and Ellen Douglas |
| 118 | relationship | Henry Douglas; sister Rebecca | Henry writing to 'sister Rebecca' | Sept. 8th 1851 | 1851-09-08 | 04-079-P01 | I have just been writing to sister Rebecca, and scolded a good deal with [[her?]] for none of you writing to me |
| 119 | place | Henry Douglas | Palestine (Anderson County) sickly for two months, more than he ever saw in Ellis Co.; Old Mr Benson | Sept 1851 | 1851-09 | 04-079-P01 | This County although reputed to be one of the healthiest in the State, has been very sickly for two months past, more so than I ever saw it in Ellis Co. I think that Old Mr Benson is nearly willing to try the [[illegible]] again |
| 120 | money | Henry Douglas | Quit Mr Dale; helping [[Hedlie?]] a large livery stable; wages $1.50 per day, board $10 per month; carpenters' opening; journeywork or undertaker | Sept 1851 | 1851-09 | 04-080-P01 | I quit working for Mr Dale about a month ago ... I am now helping [[Hedlie?]] a large Livery Stable, my wages are $1,50 cts pr day, but after subtracting $10 pr month for board & washing, the amount is considerably diminished. There is going to be a very good opening for carpenters here this coming Winter and Spring, and I am somewhat at a loss whether to continue journeywork or turn out undertaker |
| 121 | church | Christian Church at Palestine | Resolved to build a house of worship 56 by 34 feet | Sept 1851 | 1851-09 | 04-080-P01 | The Christian Church at this place have resolved to build a house of worship as soon as lumber can be procured. It will be 56 ft long & 34 wide |
| 122 | person | Mr Mallard | Bound for old Rutherford; wife wants Tennessee | Sept 1851 | 1851-09 | 04-081-P01 | I was at Mr Mallards house a time ago, he says he is bound for old Rutherford as soon as he can get off ... his wife is very much dissatisfied and cant hear to nothing else but going back to Tenn. |
| 123 | church | Susannah | Henry rejoices at Susannah's [obedience to] the Truth | Sept 1851 | 1851-09 | 04-081-P01 | Nothing gratified me more than to hear of Susannahs [[illegible]] the Truth, may she hold out faithful to the end is my sincere prayer. O that all the Family that have arrived at the years of discretion were gathered into the fold of Christ. |
| 124 | relationship | Ellen Douglas; Lizzie Douglas | Slip: to Sister Lizzie Douglas from Ellen Douglas | Jan. 12, 1862 | 1862-01-12 | 04-082-P01 | To Sister Lizzie Douglas / from / Ellen Douglas / Jan. 12, 1862 |
| 125 | event | Ellen Douglas | Christmas 1861 at her cabin: cake, pies, egg nog, 14 young people; young men left for the war | Jan. 12 1862 | 1862-01-12 | 04-083-P01 | I had plenty of cake and pies at my cabin Christmas day and a big Egg nog. ... I looked out and saw 14 young men and girls riged out comeing ... all the week after which most all the young men left for the war. |
| 126 | person | [[Gaskin?]]; Angus; Maggie; Sister Fleming; heath | Ellen's children and household | Jan 1862 | 1862-01 | 04-083-P01; 04-084-P01 | [[Gaskin?]] had a chill last week and now has very sore eyes he is walking and is as pretty as any bodys boy he can talk now as good as Angus. Maggie is growing fast and will be another Sister Fleming ... my man Angus is as fat as a pig |
| 127 | event | Warren; Beck | Warren gone to Deer Creek to move Beck there; going to the war as soon as she is confined; baby expected 5-11 Feb 1862 | Jan 1862 | 1862-01 | 04-084-P01 | Beck has not been very well ... Warren had gone to deer creek to see about moveing her there as he is going to the war as soon as she is confined they will not break up housekeeping until after that, she is expecting about the 5 to the 11 of Feb. |
| 128 | relationship | Beck (Rebecca); Warren | Warren tried to get some of her sisters to stay with her; Ellen offers | Jan 1862 | 1862-01 | 04-084-P01 | Warren tryed to get some of her sisters to come home with him and stay a while with her but they could not leave home |
| 129 | place | Ellen Douglas | Living in Sam's cabin; Sue's house commenced | Jan 1862 | 1862-01 | 04-084-P01 | we are still in Sams cabin I expect I will remain here some time yet ... Tell Sue I am glad to hear of her hopes being realized that is her house comenced |
| 130 | person | Amanda Berry; Lizzie West | Neighbours who want letters from Laura | Jan 1862 | 1862-01 | 04-085-P01 | tell Laura the last time I saw Amanda Berry she asked about her and said she must write to her Lizzie West was here not long ago |
| 131 | event | Warren; Beck | Warren will leave as soon as Beck is able to be moved to her father's | Jan 1862 | 1862-01 | 04-085-P01 | I recon you will all come now to see Warren before he goes off he will leave as soon as beck is able for him to move her to her fathers. |
| 132 | land | H. Douglas | Went to Brushy to secure 'the Land' but failed to get the certificate; dated Buchanan | Jany 15th 1862 | 1862-01-15 | 04-086-P01 | I started yesterday to Brushy to see if I could secure the Land [[illegible]] you, but failed to get the certificate him as I expected ... Buchanan Jany 15th 1862 H. Douglas |
| 133 | unit | Warren | Warren likely to get off in February; company not yet chosen | Jan 1862 | 1862-01 | 04-086-P02 | Warren is likely to get off in Feb — does not know what Co he will join yet |
| 134 | money | H. Douglas | Wool sent to Shreveport netted 26 cents | Jan 1862 | 1862-01 | 04-086-P03 | I sent [[752?]] lbs wool to Shriveport it netted 26 cts |
| 135 | relationship | Henry Douglas; Lizzie | Slip: to Sister Lizzie (possibly while in California) from Henry Douglas, Red Oak | Sept. 6, 1892 | 1892-09-06 | 04-087-P01 | To Sister Lizzie / (possibly while in California) / from Henry Douglas / Red Oak, Texas / Sept. 6, 1892 |
| 136 | church | Henry Douglas | On irreligion in California; 'California & Sodom & Gomorrah are not unlike' | Sept 6 1892 | 1892-09-06 | 04-088-P01 | I do not now wish to say anything only to regret, nay lament such a condition of irreligion as portrayed in your letter Surely California & Sodom & Gomorrah are not unlike / A day in the courts of the Lord is surely worth a thousand in such a place |
| 137 | church | Liberty; Bros Cole & Graves | Good meeting at Liberty; Clarence & Kate obeyed the gospel; they will be 14 on 8 Sept [1892] | Sept 1892 | 1892-09-08 | 04-089-P01 | We have just had a good meeting at Liberty conducted by Bros Cole & Graves / You will rejoice with me that Clarence & Kate obeyed the gospel. They will be 14 the 8th inst |
| 138 | person | Otis Maynard?; Lee Weatherford?; Robbie Sullivan?; Jesse Graves; Mrs Bur Pratts? | Others added at the meeting; 'your son' | Sept 1892 | 1892-09 | 04-090-P01 | Also your son, [[Or. Otis Maynard?]] Lee [[Weatherford?]] Robbie [[Sullivan?]] Jesse Graves & Mrs [[Bur Pratts?]] & others unknown to you |
| 139 | event | Crops 1892 | Corn good, wheat and oats fine, cotton average; Mr Ward a good renter for Lizzie | Sept 1892 | 1892-09 | 04-090-P01 | Our Wheat & Oats were fine & Cotton a good average Many are picking & the gin whistles are beginning to sound ... Mr Ward has a good crop for you I think he is a good renter |
| 140 | person | Henry Allen; Sam Strand?; Mrs Bell; Mrs Thuston?; Dr Duff; Henry Orr | Tenancy changes | Sept 1892 | 1892-09 | 04-090-P01 | Henry Allen is going to move to Hood County near Acton & Sam [[Strand?]] has rented Mrs Bells place Mrs [[Thuston?]] has rented Dr Duffs & her son in law [[Surren?]] Henry Orrs place |
| 141 | person | Jodie | In bad health; went to Erath Co.; Greer Co. crops failed | Sept 1892 | 1892-09 | 04-091-P01 | Jodie is in bad health as usual & started yesterday to Erath Co. Greer Co failed on crops this year |
| 142 | event | Billy Davis | Fell off his wagon near Palmer | Sept 1892 | 1892-09 | 04-091-P01 | Billy Davis fell off his wagon [[illegible]] [[near?]] [[Palmer?]] about 10 days ago. |
| 143 | person | Tom & Laura; John & Ann; Sue; Kate; Mr & Mrs Barton | Family in California and Brushy | Sept 1892 | 1892-09 | 04-091-P01 | Give my love to Tom & Laura & family & also to John & Ann ... Sue will probably teach in Brushy this winter & I will send Kate to her / Give to Mr & Mrs Barton / Your brother Henry |
| 144 | relationship | Henry Douglas; Mary Douglas | Slip: to Mrs Mary (N. L.) Douglas from Henry, Palestine, Dec. 30 1853 (letter reads Dec. 3d) | Dec. 30, 1853 / Dec. 3d 1853 | 1853-12-03 | 04-092-P01; 04-093-P01 | To Mrs Mary (N. L.) / Douglas / From Henry Douglas. / Palestine, Tex / Dec. 30, 1853 ... Palestine Dec. 3d 1853 / Dear Mother |
| 145 | event | Jepse; Warren | Gone out on the Brazos; Warren's second trip | Dec 1853 | 1853-12 | 04-093-P01; 04-094-P01 | I learned from him some of the particulars about home, & amongst the rest, that Jepse & Warren had gone out on / Brazos. ... Warren has been writing a little about his trip to the Brazos, but only casually alluded to his second trip |
| 146 | person | David Gaslin | Met at Bonners ferry on Trinity | Dec 1853 | 1853-11-28 | 04-093-P01 | I had however the pleasure of meeting with David Gaslin, at Bonners ferry on Trinity, last monday evening |
| 147 | church | Palestine cooperation meeting | About $450 subscribed for evangelizing; committee of 5 managers; Bro. Kendrick preached; [[6?]] baptized incl. Addison son of Bro Clark and Albert son of Bro. K | Dec 1853 | 1853-11 | 04-094-P01; 04-095-P01 | The Co-operation will, I think be quite effective; about 450 dols were subscribed for evangelizing purposes ... Bro. Kendrick had all the preaching to do. There were [[6?]] baptized & one other added to the Church 2 of them were little boys, one, Addison, son of Bro Clark aged about 12 years the other Albert, Bro. K's oldest son about 10 years old. |
| 148 | place | Mrs Mary Douglas | Address Red Oak Texas | 1853 | 1853 | 04-096-P03 | Mrs Mary Douglas / Red Oak Texas |
| 149 | relationship | Henry Douglas; N. L. Douglas | Slip: to N. L. Douglas from Henry Douglas, Palestine | Jan. 12, 1855 | 1855-01-12 | 05-002-P01 | To N. L. Douglas / from Henry Douglas / Palestine, Tex. / Jan. 12, 1855 |
| 150 | event | Henry Douglas | Left home in October [1854]; decided to remain in Palestine one more year; partnership with Dr Kendrick, one-third of accounts and expense | Jan. 12 1855 | 1855-01-12 | 05-003-P01 | I concluded to remain here one year longer, & risk the consequences. Dr Kendrick & myself have formed a partnership for the year. I get ⅓ of the accounts, & am at ⅓ the expence, pay my board & furnish my own horse |
| 151 | person | Old Mr Ransom | Known to N. L. Douglas; will try Dr K. | Jan 1855 | 1855-01 | 05-004-P01 | Old Mr Ransom, (whom I take to be a man of strong prejudices) has declared his intention to try Dr K when next he wants a physician. ... I mention him, as he is known to you |
| 152 | event | Henry Douglas | Wrote to Warren of a cancer case; promises a visit in April | Jan 1855 | 1855-01 | 05-004-P01 | I wrote to Warren, of a Cancer case we have on hand ... I think I can venture to promise you a visit in the Spring, say April. |
| 153 | relationship | Henry Douglas; Mary Douglas; Susannah | Letter from Mary and Susannah; Uncle Sam's court engagements; Jepie's and Warren's absences; Susannah to come down with Henry | Ap. 8, 1853 | 1853-04-08 | 05-006-P01 | The letter from yourself & Susannah, came by last mail ... I recd a letter from Uncle Sam a short time ago, suggesting the 1st of May ... in consequence of his engagements, at court in April & also Jepie's & Warrens absences ... I am pleased that Susannah has consented to come down with me |
| 154 | event | Henry Douglas; N. L. Douglas | Parting from Father near the Willow pond; walked to Porters Bluff | before Apr 1853 |  | 05-007-P01 | I know that while memory lasts I shall never forget the day when I parted from Father, in the wide lonely prairie, near the Willow pond, his parting advice, & the loneliness of the subsequent hours in walking to Porters Bluff. Never till then had I felt so isolated; never had I felt what it was to be homeless, houseless, & in effect, friendless |
| 155 | event | Weather 1853 | Dry; corn frostbitten; wheat feared ruined | Apr 1853 | 1853-04 | 05-007-P01 | The weather has been very dry with us, for a long time, & crops are suffering greatly for rain. Corn has been badly frostbitten ... I fear the wheat has been ruined, with you. |
| 156 | relationship | aunt Cynthia | Postscript: remember me to [[Un.?]] & aunt Cynthia | Apr 1853 | 1853-04 | 05-007-P02 | P.S. Give my love to all. Remember me to [[Un.?]] & aunt Cynthia. &c &c / H.D. |
| 157 | event | Henry Douglas | Slip: driving a herd of sheep to Texas from Mississippi County, Missouri | June 23, 1860 | 1860-06-23 | 05-008-P01 | To N. L. Douglas / from / Henry Douglas / Mississippi County, / Missouri / June 23, 1860 / — re driving herd of / sheep to Texas |
| 158 | money | Henry Douglas; Willis Helm | Paid $1-2 per sheep; Willis Helm sent $500; with $1500 bought 750 head; about $200 left | June 23d 1860 | 1860-06-23 | 05-009-P01 | I have had to pay from 1 to 2 dollars for sheep ... Mr Willis Helm sent $500 by me to get him some sheep & with the 1500 I have bought 750 head & do not know that I will have money enough to get them to Tex. as I only have some 200 left. |
| 159 | place | Henry Douglas | Bought sheep in Callaway & Graves Co's Ky and Christian Co; crossed the Mississippi at the Iron Banks; ferriage 8 cts per head | June 1860 | 1860-06 | 05-009-P01 | I bought common sheep in Callaway & Graves Co's Ky purchase lambs at 1 & Ewes at 1.25 per head ... went back to Christian Co & got some 250 head at 1.75 & 2 per head ... I crossed the Mississippi at the Iron Banks last Sunday & had to pay 8 cts per head ferriage |
| 160 | event | Sheep drove | Lost near 50 head; disease; a drove from Rutherford Co Tennessee passed | June 1860 | 1860-06 | 05-009-P01; 05-010-P01 | Several have died & altogether I have lost near 50 head since I started ... A few days ago a drove of 7 or 800 passed from Rutherford Co Tennessee & lost a great many. |
| 161 | money | Henry Douglas | 4 hands hired at $15 per month; route via Fort Gibson ferry on the Arkansas and Springfield Mo. | June 1860 | 1860-06 | 05-011-P01 | I have 4 hands hired at 15 dollars per month each & taking all together I cannot make the drove profitable if I do not lose by it. I have not heard from any of you since I left Texas nor have I heard from Ellen since I left Lincoln County. ... I suppose I will reach Springfield Mo. in 30 or 40 days |
| 162 | person | Ellen | Henry has not heard from Ellen since leaving Lincoln County | June 1860 | 1860-06 | 05-011-P01 | nor have I heard from Ellen since I left Lincoln County |
| 163 | money | Samuel Wallace; N. L. Douglas | Promissory note: five barrels corn or four dollars by 25 Dec next | 2nd Oct. 1832 | 1832-10-02 | 05-012-P02 | By the 25th December next I promise to pay to N L Douglas at his house five barrels corn, or four Dollars, value recd this 2nd Oct. 1832. / Samuel Wallace [[seal]] |
| 164 | event | James Bass; N. L. Douglas | Business letter from Murfreesboro; two to three years since he heard from Douglas about 'our business' | April 18 /54 | 1854-04-18 | 05-014-P01 | It has been about two years since I heard from you in relation to our business — it has been I believe nearly three years. |
| 165 | land | James Bass; Gen. A. Sublett | Bass bought the balance of the Littleton White tract, about 3250 acres; wants Douglas to see whether taxes are paid; Louis Dickson tract on the Trinity at the mouth of five mile creek | April 1854 | 1854-04-18 | 05-014-P01 | I have bought lately of Gen. A. Sublett the balance of the Littleton White tract of Land — the purchase embraces about thirty two hundred & fifty acres — You know where it is situated & I want you to see to it. See whether the Taxes are paid ... the Louis Dickson tract of Land on the Trinity River ... I believe the Land is at the mouth of the five mile [[deleted: branch?]] [[inserted: creek]] |

N L Douglas as agent for G A Sublett has this day paid me the amount of Tax and Cost of Sale and one hundred per cent there and as required by Law for the Redemption of 1280 acres of Land lying on Subletts Creek and sold by me for the Taxes for the year 1848 [[inserted: and listed to A J Porter]] on the fourth day of February AD 1850 and purchased by C M Winkler ... W. B. McCabe [[seal]] / assessor & collector / for the county Navarro ... I ratify and sanction the doings of W. B. McCabe ... Jany 31st 1851 / C M Winkler |

| 176 | event | Sadie Headrick | Letter from Bardsdale; 'haven't had a break down since I wrote last'; sitting-room furnishings; the store | Sep, 29th, 1893 | 1893-09-29 | 05-036-P01; 05-036-P02; 05-036-P03 | Bardsdale, Sep, 29th, 1893. / My Dear Aunt:—I finished up my morning work pretty well this forenoon ... I am still better—haven't had a "break down" since I wrote last. |
| 177 | person | Bruce; Elmo; Mr. Headrick | Sadie's children Bruce and Elmo; Mr. Headrick down with his back; Grandma [[Chadens?]]'s house; Mrs. [[Scholl?]] to Kansas | Sept 1893 | 1893-09 | 05-037-P01 | One day he came from the P.O. & said "Grandma [[Chadens?]] house isn't togezzer is it Mamma?" ... Mr. Headrick has been down with his back, for two weeks ... Mrs. [[Scholl?]] started back to Kansas the 19th. |
| 178 | event | Sadie Headrick | Dreamed of the old homestead, telling 'you & Grandma my troubles', and of Uncle [[George?]] | Sept 1893 | 1893-09 | 05-038-P01 | I dreamed, last night of being at the old homestead, & telling you & Grandma my troubles, as I was wont to do in by-gone days & also of seeing Uncle [[George?]] ... A few nights before I dreamed of Grandma. |
| 179 | event | Elmo | Elmo's joke about Mr. [[McKie's?]] cow | Sept 1893 | 1893-09 | 05-039-P01 | along came Mr. [[McKie's?]] cow—Elmo remarked if that cow was married, she would have a calf. (with her he meant) Making that a [[test?]] most of the California cows are unmarried. |
| 180 | church | Advocate; D. L.; Uncle Geo.; Bro. Polly | Article in the Advocate of April 13 by D. L.; Uncle Geo.'s announcement of the [[Elmwood?]] camp-meeting; Bro. Polly's meetings | 1893 | 1893 | 05-040-P01 | There is a thought contained in the piece headed "A [[Lyffe?]]" (in Advocate of April 13th) by D. L. ... Saw in my last paper, the announcement, by Uncle Geo. of the [[Elmwood?]] camp-meeting—How I wish I could attend as of old. Remember me to Bro. Polly |
| 181 | person | Kate [[Larson?]]; Mr. H. | Kate stayed while Mr. H. went to Los Angeles; 20 dozen eggs to count | 1893 | 1893 | 05-041-P01 | Last week Mr. H. went to Los Angeles—Kate [[Larson?]] staid with me, Bruce was sick with cold & fever ... in came over 20 doz. eggs to be counted |
| 182 | church | Sadie Headrick | Refuses life insurance on religious convictions | 1893 | 1893 | 05-043-P01 | a Life Insurance Agent & tried to get me to influence Mr. H. to insure. I told him 'twas against my religious convictions He said "christianity would never keep you from starving to death besides the majority of christians favored it" I told him the "majority" were on their way to perdition too. |
| 183 | person | Mrs. Timmons; Ellen | Mrs. Timmons abed with nervous prostration; 'Now you & Ellen shake your sides' | 1893 | 1893 | 05-044-P01; 05-044-P02 | Now you & Ellen shake your sides. / Last monday week, I went up to Mrs. Timmons & found her abed with nervous prostration |
