| 185 | person | Grandma Humphrey; Dr. [[Hinckley?]]; Drs. Bard and Mott | Grandma Humphrey's growth; operation | August 8th |  | 05-047-P01 | Grandma Humphrey's growth increases, and is so bad she has difficulty in breathing—consequently has consented to have an operation performed ... Dr. [[Hinckley?]] is back now and he Drs. Bard and Mott, are to do the work. |
| 186 | event | Picnic at Mr. Grimes' | Mr. Wigmore's cart; Mrs. Pickering; Mr. Henry Chadsey in Mrs. [[Curren's?]] surrey; Lillie Chadsey | August |  | 05-047-P02; 05-048-P01 | We had our picnic at Mr. Grimes, last saturday ... if I didn't go down and ask Mr. Wigmore for his cart ... I found Mrs. Pickering hardly able to be out of bed ... down came Mr. Henry Chadsey in Mrs. [[Curren's?]] surrey, for us. Lillie Chadsey had inquired of several why I wasn't there ... They had lemonade and ice-cream. two swings a set of croquet, and a game of baseball. |
| 187 | event | little [[Beckie's?]] | Married at Santa Paula on her 18th birthday to Mr. [[Reck?]] [[Lehock?]] unbeknown to her father; Grandma Roberts | the 4th |  | 05-048-P01 | That was the 4th, and little [[Beckie's?]] birthday, and what think you, she went to Santa Paula with Mr. [[Reck?]] [[Lehock?]] and married him unbeknowing to her father. ... It was her 18th birthday. ... Grandma Roberts spent last sunday night with us. |
| 188 | money | Sadie Headrick | Earned $4.50 pitting fruit | August |  | 05-048-P01 | Well I must tell you that I earned $4.50 pitting fruit. A handsome amount for me. |
| 189 | place | Mrs. Sara Headrick | Envelope to Mrs. Sara Headrick, Red Oak, Texas; postage due 2 cents; postmark [[SANTA BARBARA CAL.?]] APR 27 1921 | APR 27 1921 | 1921-04-27 | 05-049-P01; 05-049-P02 | Mrs. Sara Headrick / Red Oak. / Texas. / POSTAGE DUE 2 CENTS / [[SANTA BARBARA CAL.?]] APR 27 6:30 PM 1921 |
| 190 | person | Mrs. Weatherford; Cousin Sarah Jeter | Warning about 'that son of hers' |  |  | 05-050-P01 | I think I have heard Mrs. Weatherford speak of Cousin Sarah Jeter, and I must caution you right here, not to allow your-self to become too much infatuated with that son of hers |
| 191 | money | Mr. [[Annis?]]; Clapp ranch; church organ | Clapp ranch bought for $1500; $35 subscribed and $50 from Mr. Bard for a new church organ |  |  | 05-050-P01 | Mr. [[Annis?]] has bought the Clapp ranch over the mountains. Payed $1500. When Mr. [[Clapp?]] left, he sold the church organ—So Kate Burdick Wheeler & Mary Yeager, set to work to raise money to buy another. They succeeded in getting $35 subscribed & Mr. Bard, who owns so much land here, sent a check for $50 |
| 192 | church | Mr. Pickering; Holiness sermon | Bible study proposal; Holiness sermon on sanctification and quitting tobacco |  |  | 05-051-P01 | Mr. Pickering, our Supt and the only one in the School, who knows anything about the Bible, suggested, that we older ones get together once a week, and study by subjects ... Last sunday we had a Holiness sermon ... the crowning point of his being sanctified was quitting tobacco. |
| 193 | church | Mrs. [[Wigart?]] | Does not think water baptism essential; expects the spirit as a dove |  |  | 05-053-P01 | she doesn't think water baptism, at all, essential to salvation; but still she thinks perhaps, sometime, she will be immersed—adding, she always had an idea if she ever did, the spirit would descend on her, in the shape of a dove |
| 194 | person | Fannie | Slip: part of a letter from Fannie mentioning Lizzie & Laura; page 4 mentions Johnson Co. and [[Evan?]] |  |  | 05-054-P01; 05-056-P02; 05-056-P05 | Part of letter – / Mentions / Lizzie & Laura / from Fannie ... one of the prettiest looking [[little?]] [[fellows?]] that left Johnson Co. ... I must to write a letter to [[Evan?]] to night. |

| 197 | event | George Douglas | Slip: 'Concerning Uncle George Douglas – his work at Sylvanna Church – his death Sept. 23, 1923' | Sept. 23, 1923 | 1923-09-23 | 05-060-P01 | Concerning Uncle / George Douglas / – his work at / Sylvanna Church / – his death / Sept. 23, 1923 |
| 198 | event | George Douglas | Continued his labors with the Church at Sylvana until his death 23 Sept 1923; opposed the Sunday School but did not withdraw; died in Georgia | Sept 23 1923 | 1923-09-23 | 05-062-P01 | My father continued his labors with the Church at Sylvana until his death Sept 23 1923. Though opposing the Sunday School he did not withdraw from the congregation but endeavored to present the truth as he understood it. His health became impaired and he realized that his work was done. He died in Ga and |
| 199 | event | George Douglas; Sylvana congregation | Brought back and buried from the Church; strong character; after his death the congregation ceased to meet | after 1923 |  | 05-061-P01; 05-061-P02 | was brought back and buried from the Church that had been his life work. / He was a strong character and was [[inserted: in full sense an excellent leader]] While in his prime to direct the affairs of the Church, he was unable to train anyone to take his place and after his death the congregation was torn by dissensions and after a few years ceased to consistently meet |
| 200 | event | Poem | 'In Answer to the Author of "Georgie"' (manuscript verse) |  |  | 05-063-P01; 05-066-P01; 05-066-P02 | In answer to the Author of "Georgie." / I seek not for devoted dears.— / "Platonic friendship at my years," / Most surely will suffice |

Wright (Alexander); Mary Wright | Slip: Mrs (Mary) Douglas and Cynthia Orr are daughters of Rebecca Goodloe; Jacob Wright brother of Rebecca Wright Goodloe; Jacob Wright and wife were cousins; Rebecca Alexander their daughter | Dec. 6, 1883 | 1883-12-06 | 05-086-P01 | To Mrs. (Mary) Douglas & / Cynthia Orr – daughters / of Rebecca Goodloe / from / B. F. Lillard / Murfreesboro, Tenn. / Dec. 6, 1883 / [Regarding decision of Circuit Court judge in suit of nephews & neices of Mrs. Alexander, / (Rebecca T. Wright) / Jacob Wright, brother of Rebecca Wright Goodloe.] / – Decree appealed by Mr. Lillard – / – Shows Jacob Wright & Wife were cousins – / Rebecca Alexander their daughter, being equal heir of the father & mother of Mary Wright. |

| 207 | event | Chancellor Burton | Sick and died during the term; a Circuit Court judge decided the case | Dec 1883 | 1883-12 | 05-087-P01 | During the Term of the Court, Chancellor Burton was sick and died. While he was sick a Circuit Court Judge held his Court and decided the case as to who the land goes to, but seemed very much tangled in the case. |
| 208 | land | Rebecca Alexander estate | 180 acres from her father Jacob Wright to his ~40 nephews and nieces (17 living, 23 dead); the McGlothlin tract of 230/235 acres from her mother 'ascends' to heirs of Mary Wright; parents were cousins | Dec 1883 | 1883-12 | 05-087-P02; 05-088-P01 | He decided that 180 Acres of the land that descended to Mrs. Alexander from her father, Jacob Wright, goes to or is inherited by the nephews and nieces of Jacob Wright ... as he had about forty nephews and nieces, seventeen of whom are living, and about twenty three dead ... As to the McGlothlin tract of 230 Acres, descended to Mrs. Alexander from her mother ... the father and mother being cousins |
| 209 | event | B. F. Lillard | Appealed to the Supreme Court; 'Per Ida' | Dec 6 1883 | 1883-12-06 | 05-088-P01 | I appealed from the whole decree to the Supreme Court as also did the McGlothlin claimants ... Very Respectfully, / B. F. Lillard. / Per Ida. |
| 210 | land | Mary Douglas; B. F. Lillard | Draft power of attorney: Mary Douglas of Ellis County appoints Lillard to demand her distributive share in the real estate descended to her from Mrs Rebecca T. Alexander, late of Rutherford County, Tennessee | [[blank]] 1883 | 1883 | 05-090-P01 | I Mrs Mary Douglas of said County and State do hereby nominate and appoint B. F. Lillard of Murfreesboro Tenn, my true and lawful Attorney ... the distributive share or interest in the real estate that descended to me from Mrs Rebecca T. Alexander, late of Rutherford County and State of Tennessee, now deceased |
| 211 | relationship | M. M. Dill; George Douglas; Cynthia Orr; Docia Bivins; Isaac N. Wright; Mrs. Nance; Mandy | Dill ('Your Cousin') writes to George at Ferris; heirs named | Feby 26th 1883 | 1883-02-26 | 05-092-P01; 05-093-P02 | Mr Lillard our Attorney has shown me a letter from you in answer to two letters that I requested him to write to your Mother and Cousin Cynthia Orr ... I have conferred with Cousin Docia Bivins, she stated that she was going to leave her part, I have also conferred with Isaac N. Wright ... he has recd a letter from Mrs Nance ... Isaac Wright recd a letter from his sister Mandy ... Your Cousin / M M Dill. |
| 212 | land | Rebecca Alexander estate | 640 acre tract sold by Alexander & wife; the River Tract & the Arkansas lands | Feb 1883 | 1883-02 | 05-093-P02 | We will not at present take any steps against the 640 Acre Tract of land sold by Alexander & wife ... The River Tract & the Arkansas lands will no doubt be surrendered up |
| 213 | relationship | Mary Douglas; Rebecca Alexander; Jacob Wright | Lillard: Mrs Alexander was 'your Cousin ... daughter of your uncle Jacob Wright'; heirs are first cousins: 3 Goodloe, 3 Dill, 2 Brasher, 6 Thompson Wright | Dec. 30/82 | 1882-12-30 | 05-095-P01 | I suppose that you have heard of the death of your Cousin Mrs Alexander daughter of your uncle Jacob Wright. She did not make a will and her real estate descends to her next of kin consisting of her first Cousins now living being 3 of the Goodloe family, three of the Dill, two of the Brasher and six of the Thompson Wright family |
| 214 | money | Rebecca Alexander estate | 680 acres assessed at $8500; probably 300 acres in Arkansas; 640 acres sold for $6400 on 1-3 years' credit; conveyance defective | Dec 1882; Feby 11th 1883 | 1882-12-30; 1883-02-11 | 05-095-P01; 05-096-P02; 05-096-P03 | the real estate consisting of 680 Acres of land assessed at $8500.00 but of course land generally brings double the assessment ... probably 300 Acres in Ark. ... The 640 Acres sold as above referred to was sold in order to convert the property as I have been informed for $6400.00 rather a depreciated price on one two and three years credit ... The conveyance is defective |
| 215 | place | Mary Douglas | Post office corrected from Ennis to Ferris; letter returned after 28 days | Feby 11th 1883 | 1883-02-11 | 05-095-P01; 05-096-P02 | [[deleted: Ennis]] [[inserted: Ferris]] Texas ... The above letter was mailed and directed to Ennis and after 28 days returned to me. I now learn that your Post Office is Ferris |
| 216 | event | B. F. Lillard; George Douglas | George's letter of 15 March 1883 questioned Lillard; Lillard's defence; twenty-five years at the bar; Judge Peter Turney of Winchester; Judge Burton; John Woods; Col. N. D. Robinson; whisky prescribed for neuralgic rheumatism from army wounds | March 22nd 1883 | 1883-03-22 | 05-098-P01; 05-100-P01; 05-102-P01; 05-102-P02 | Your letter of March 15th has been recd. I think that you have been under a misapprehension about several things ... I have referred you to Judge Peter Turney of Winchester Tenn ... my Law Diploma was signed 25 years ago by Judges Green & Caruthers of Cumberland University ... whisky for neuralgic rheumatism ... caused from Army wounds |
| 217 | person | Albert Jetton | 'your Cousin Albert Jetton whose store is within 75-ft of that Law Office'; recommends Lillard as 'Your Nephew' | Feb 12th /83; Feb 14th /83 | 1883-02-12; 1883-02-14 | 05-103-P01; 05-110-P01; 05-117-P02 | Why did not Mr. Alexander bring into the Consultation your Cousin Albert Jetton whose store is within 75-ft of that Law Office ... I take pleasure in recommending him to you / Your Nephew Albert Jetton ... I do not advise a Suit but only recommend Mr Lillard as a Lawyer / Yours A Jetton |
| 218 | land | D Travis; A. M. Alexander; D. M. McKnight | 640 acre tract conveyed to D Travis for $6400 in three payments Aug 1882-84; acknowledged 15 July 1881; privy examination by McKnight, a half brother of A. M. Alexander; registered Sept 1882; Rebecca Alexander died 11 Nov 1882 | 2–26–83 | 1883-02-26 | 05-107-P01 | The 640 Acre Tract of land was conveyed by deed to D Travis for the Consideration of $6400.00 to be paid in three equal payments Augst 1882, 83, 84 – It was not dated but that is obviated by the date of the acknowledgement before the Clerk July 15–1881, by A. M. Alexander – on the same day a Commission was issued by the Clerk to D. M. McKnight a Justice of the Peace and a half brother of A. M. Alexander to take the Privy Examination of Mrs Alexander ... This deed was registered Sept, 1882, and she died Nov, 11 1882, |

| 220 | event | L Jetton; Dr. Dickson; A. M. Alexander | Lillard 'too fast'; Alexander wants no suit; 'A. M. A will do right' | Feb 12th 1883 | 1883-02-12 | 05-111-P01; 05-111-P02; 05-113-P01 | I learn from Dr. Dickson to day that Mr Alexander dont want any Suit that if there be no will he will do any thing that is right ... I believe A. M. A will do right – he has always been a Cleaver Man |
| 221 | event | J. N. Spence; Mrs Bivins; A. M. Alexander | Spence, son-in-law of Mrs Bivins: Alexander will give up the lands if no will; Rebecca's father on his death bed said he made no will | [15th, 1883] | 1883 | 05-115-P01; 05-115-P02 | P. S. J. N. Spence the soninlaw of Mrs Bivins informed me that Mr A. M. Alexander about two weeks ago said that if they found no will that he did not want any lawsuit ... she said that her Father on his death bed told her that he made no will, and asked her to promise the same and that she said that she had so promised and that she did not want Mr Alexander's kin to have her property |
| 222 | relationship | L. A. Jetton; Susanna (Sue) Bell | Slip: to Mrs. Susanna (Sue) Bell from Cousin L. A. Jetton / (Lewis?) / Readyville, Tenn. / Feb. 20, 1870 / (Tells of death of Polly Wright) |
| 223 | event | L A Jetton | Very sick; Dr. Briges; Aunt Clark; Legislature to adjourn by 15 March; Sallie Wieden has had three [[Divorces?]]; John's wife has another boy | Feb 20th 1870 | 1870-02-20 | 05-119-P01; 05-119-P02; 05-120-P01 | Dr. Briges who Attend me during my sickness Thinks My illness was brought on by cold ... Aunt Clark has been with us several days ... It is Thought The Legislature will Adjurn by the 15th of march ... Sallie Wieden ... She tells us that She has had Three [[Divorces?]] but is as lively as a cricket ... Johns wife has another fine boy Three weeks old |
| 224 | event | Old Aunt Polly Wright | Died about two weeks before 20 Feb 1870 | about two weeks ago | 1870-02 | 05-120-P01 | Old Aunt Polly Wright died about two weeks ago She died I suppose with old age |
| 225 | relationship | Sarah (Tullison); Smith (Popley); [[Temith?]]; Marier ([[Darnum?]] [[Vaught?]]); [[Shonce?]]; Mary; Joseph; Mr Akers; Aunt Jane | 'The girls' and whom they married | Feb 1870 | 1870-02 | 05-120-P02; 05-121-P01 | Sarah married a drunken trifling fellow by the name of Tullison They have been married fifteen years and have eight living children ... Smith Married a man name Popley ... Marier is the looking and the smartest one of the family She married [[Darnum?]] [[Vaught?]] he was killed shortly after we came here in a drunken spree ... Mary is still Single ... Joseph is married and lives in the city ... Mr Akers Says about his [[Soninlaw?]] he Says The devil owed him a grudge and has paid him off in Soninlaws ... Aunt Jane also looks very old |
| 226 | person | Pap; Ellen; Cousin Ellen | L A Jetton's Pap complains of poverty; love to Ellen | Feb 1870 | 1870-02 | 05-121-P01 | Pap is always Complaining about his poverty and That he has a hard way to get along and he is afraid he will come to Starvation ... tell Ellen to write to Mary Give my love to Cousin Ellen |
| 227 | relationship | Jessie Douglas; George & Lizzie Douglas; Earl Stuart Douglas | Slip: to George & Lizzie from Jessie Douglas, Cross Creeks, Tex., announcing birth of Earl Stuart Douglas | Aug. 30, 1869 | 1869-08-30 | 05-122-P01 | To George & Lizzie / Douglas / from / Jessie Douglas / Cross Creeks, Tex. / Aug. 30, 1869 / Announces birth of / Earl Stuart Douglas |
| 228 | event | Jesse Douglas | Cross Creek, Travis County; flood; sickness; tried to rent nearer Austin; dissatisfied with the Mountains; had determined to move to Arkansas but mother-in-law's tears prevent | August the 30th 1869 | 1869-08-30 | 05-123-P01; 05-123-P02; 05-123-P04 | Cross Creek Travis Texas / August the 30th 1869 ... I was fully determined to move to Arkansas this fall. But I must Confess that the grief and tears of a good Old motherinlaw is a preventative, at least as long as She lives and possibly She may out live Me. |
| 229 | event | Earl Stewart Douglas | Born 2 Aug 1869 weighing ten pounds; [[Ma?]] up two weeks | 2 August 1869 | 1869-08-02 | 05-124-P02 | tell Tom and Laura that Dubly Beats Me, But I must say that on the second of August Earl Stewart Douglas made his appearance Weighing ten pounds he is growing finely and [[Ma?]] has been up two weeks doing her work. |
| 230 | money | Jesse Douglas | Crops 1869: grass total loss, corn half rotten, cotton a third; 300 bushels sound corn; will make about 1 bag of cotton; McCluer picked 5-6 thousand off eight acres | Sept the 15th 1869 | 1869-09-15 | 05-124-P01; 05-124-P03; 05-125-P03 | My grass was a total loss; corn ½ rotten my Cotton I thought at first was not injured But time tells it, I think maybe one third of a crop ... Will make or have 300 hundred Bushels sound Corn to My Share ... I fear from the progress of my Cotton in the way of its opening I wont make more than 1 Bag. McCluer off of about Eight Acres has already picked Between 5 and 6 Thousand |
| 231 | person | Sarah | 'tell Sarah I am coming to see her this Winter or fall' | Sept 1869 | 1869-09 | 05-125-P06 | tell Sarah I am coming to see her this Winter or fall |

| 236 | money | Jesse Douglas | Crop one bale of 434 lbs which paid debts and left one dollar; about thirty dollars behind after medicine and doctor's bill | Nov 1869 | 1869-11 | 05-130-P04 | Well My [[lost: torn edge]]rop amounted to one Bale [[lost: torn edge]] 434 lbs which paid My debts [[lost: torn edge]] Left me one dollar, the sale and [[lost: torn edge]]ttlement previous to sickness — [[lost: torn edge]] medicine & Dr Bill since Leaves Me about thirty dollars Behind, that I will be able to settle with the sale of Pork this winter |
| 237 | relationship | Jesse Douglas; the Old Stewarts | Cannot move during 'the Old Stewarts life time'; George to 'make your figures In regard the Old Farm' | Nov 1869 | 1869-11 | 05-131-P02 | It is out of the question Me to move during the Old Stewarts life time I think She would go crasy. She is old and frail in body and mind ... As I Cant Mo[[lost: torn edge]] from this Country I will Leave [[lost: torn edge]] to make your figures In regard the Old Farm |
