| 271 | person | Willie; Mary Lee; Granville; Pap; Adele; Bro Mcguffy; Jennie; Aunt Sallie | L. A. Jetton's household 1883: Willie; Mary Lee's babe Granville sick; Pap improved; Adele's new home; Bro Mcguffy moved; Jennie half a mile; Aunt Sallie in West Tenn. | March 20 1883 | 1883-03-20 | 06-013-P01; 06-014-P01 | Willie & myself are both as well as usual ... Mary Lees Babe little Granville has been very sick but is better to day Paps health has improved ... Adele spent Several days with me last week ... Bro Mcguffy has moved to his new home ... I am very glad to have Jennie So near me it is only a half a mile ... Aunt Sallie is still in west Tenn |
| 272 | event | L. A. Jetton | Lonely 'only a little negro with me'; Willie works his grandpa's land and hauls | March 1883 | 1883-03 | 06-014-P01 | I have Such a lonely time here only a little negro with me Willie is at home of nights but gone every day he is working land over at his Gran Paps this year |
| 273 | event | Cousin Rebecca Alexander | Death from cold caught looking at the comet; in her right mind; died the evening after eating breakfast | before March 1883 (Nov 11 1882 per Lillard) | 1882-11-11 | 06-014-P02 | She exposed herself getting up very early of mornings to look at the Comet which She was very much interested about She took a very Severe Cold and died from the effect of it I think She was in her right mind all the time during her Sickness Sat up eat her breakfast and talked with her friends in the morning and died that evening |
| 274 | event | Cousin Andy (A. M. Alexander); Charlie; Pap | Will stolen from her drawer two weeks before her death; Andy willing to give up the land, wants to buy from the heirs; Jacob Wright estate worth about $[[26000?]] | March 1883 | 1883-03 | 06-015-P01 | he told them Cousin Rebecca will was stolen out of her draw two weeks before her death he is willing to give up the land does not wish to have a law Suit but wants to buy the land of the heirs has already made arangements to get the interest of Several of the Wright family The Jacob Wright Estate is said to be worth about $[[26000?]] and my own opinion is the heirs ought to have it as Cousin Andy will be a rich man without it |
| 275 | relationship | L. A. Jetton; Lizzie Douglas | Slip: March 3 1878, mentions death[?] of Susanna Douglas Bell | March 3, 1878 | 1878-03-03 | 06-016-P01 | To Miss Lizzie / Douglas / from Cousin / L. A. Jetton / Readyville, Tenn / March 3, 1878 / (mentions death[?] / of Susanna Douglas bell) |
| 276 | event | L. A. Jetton household 1878 | Pap & Mother complaining; children have whooping cough; Willie bilious fever; Adele; Cousin Nellie; Charles's children; wedding and reception at Cousin Miriam's; Jimmie & Mary; newhope church | March the 3 1878 | 1878-03-03 | 06-017-P01 | Pap & Mother are both Complaining and the Children all have the Whooping Cough ... Well the wedding Came off at the appointed time ... Adele Willie and myself all went to the reception At Cousin Miriams ... Jimmie & Mary are going to live with Cousin Miriam ... they have [[inserted: gone]] to Church to day at newhope |
| 277 | event | Cousin Sue (Susanna Bell) | Condolence: 'I loved Cousin Sue on earth I hope to meet her in heaven' | March 1878 | 1878-03 | 06-018-P01 | Dear Cousin you have my deepest sympathy in your great afflictions I loved Cousin Sue on earth I hope to meet her in heaven where Sorrows and partings are unknown |
| 278 | person | may beth; uncle Jimmy; Cousin S; Adele | Jimmie chosen 'in fun' as son-in-law; uncle Jimmy to get pictures | March 1878 | 1878-03 | 06-018-P01 | I told may beth I had picked him out for my Soninlaw (just in fun you know) ... Tell uncle Jimmy Mary is going to write to him before long and Send him hers & Jimmies pictures ... Adele says tell [[Cousin S he?]] will write to her |
| 279 | relationship | Maybel ? Jetton; Lizzie Douglas | Slip: to Lizzie Douglas from Maybel ? Jetton, Readyville; signature read [[Maybey?]] Jetton | Apr. 8, 1874 | 1874-04-08 | 06-019-P01; 06-022-P01 | To / Lizzie Douglas / from / Maybel ? / Jetton / Readyville, Tenn / Apr. 8, 1874 ... Yours Truly / [[Maybey?]] / Jetton |
| 280 | person | aunt Nellie; aunt Sarah; uncle Charlie; uncle Tip; Grandpap; Mr. Barr; Nannie Fletcher; Dr. Dickson; aunt Jane | Readyville kin 1874; uncle Charlie weighs 200 pounds; Mr. Barr's youngest son to marry Miss Nannie Fletcher, niece of Dr. Dickson | April. 8th 1874 | 1874-04-08 | 06-020-P01; 06-021-P01 | aunt Nellie. She has been very sick ... She is afraid she has the consumption. aunt Sarah was over a few days ago ... uncle Charlie has grown / to be so large I dont expect you would hardly know him he weighs two hundred pounds. uncle Tip Started to Nashville for his new Spring goods last monday. Grandpap went with him ... Mr. Barrs youngest Son is to be married to morrow night to Miss Nannie Fletcher neice of Dr. Dicksons ... I suppose aunt Jane and Mr. Barr will be very well pleased |
| 281 | church | new hope Church; Ma; uncle Albert | Preaching first Sunday monthly; Sunday School every Sunday evening; Ma and uncle Albert teachers; church members dancing | April 1874 | 1874-04 | 06-021-P01; 06-021-P02 | Several of our church members have been / dancing. We have preaching at new hope Church the first Sunday in every month and Sunday School every Sunday evening. Ma and uncle Albert are teachers |
| 282 | person | Jennie White Jetton; Delie; Willie; Joanna; Cousin George | Children at school; scrapbook; asks Cousin George for verses | April 1874 | 1874-04 | 06-021-P02 | Little Jennie White Jetton is Staying [[deleted: here]] going to school. She and Delie have a fine time to gether ... Joanna has had her [[deleted: illegible]] flower garden made ... I have Started me a Scrapbook tell Cousin George to Send me some pretty verses to |
| 283 | event | Maybel/Marybee Jetton | Longs to see 'my old home and friends' (i.e. Texas) | April 1874 | 1874-04 | 06-022-P01 | Dear cousin you dont know how much I should like to see my old home and friends once more. I thought when I first came away I could soon forget you all; but not so |
| 284 | relationship | L A Jetton (Lewis ?); Lizzie Douglas | Slip: to Miss Lizzie Douglas from Cousin L A Jetton (Lewis ?), Readyville | Feb 1, 1874 | 1874-02-01 | 06-023-P01 | To Miss Lizzie / Douglas / from Cousin / L A Jetton / (Lewis ?) / Readyville, Tenn. / Feb 1, 1874 |
| 285 | church | Readyville school; Bro Rice Sewell; newhope | School taught by a preaching brother; Bro Rice Sewell preaches at newhope once a month; three other churches | Feb. 1 1874 | 1874-02-01 | 06-024-P01 | The school will be taught at Readyville by one of our preaching brethren he has been teaching for many years and is a Splendid scholar and a good man We have preaching at newhope once a month Bro Rice Sewell preaches for us again this year |
| 286 | money | Lizzie Douglas; L A Jetton | Lizzie sent money from her Ma's sale of the saddle | Feb 1874 | 1874-02 | 06-025-P01 | I was very much surprised when I saw the Money you sent me I never dreamed of such a thing as your Ma selling the saddle but as you say money never comes amiss |
| 287 | person | Mr Barr; Aunt Jane; Marybee; Charlie; Alma; Eddie Jolly; Emma; Peter | Readyville kin, 1874; Charlie's wife disliked; Eddie Jolly courting Marybee | Feb 1874 | 1874-02 | 06-025-P01; 06-025-P02 | Mr Barr and Aunt Jane Came up and Spent a night with us ... Marybee went to Readyville last week and [[deleted: illegible]] her picture taken ... Charlie and family are all getting on finely Little Alma is a sweet little thing ... I have less use for his lady than any woman I ever saw ... Mr Eddie Jolly one of Emmas brothers is coming to see Marybee I dont know what Peter will say about it |
| 288 | relationship | Fannie (Columbia Goodloe); Tommie, French, Bobbie, Mary; Newton Goodloe; Mary Douglas; Cynthia Orr | Slip: Fannie ? (Columbia Goodloe) mentions brothers Tommie (Thomas), French, Bobbie (Robert), sister Mary, children of Newton Goodloe, the brother of Mary Douglas & Cynthia Orr; written from Russellville, Ala. | June 21, 1874 | 1874-06-21 | 06-026-P01 | To Cousin / Lizzie Douglas / from / Fannie ? / (Columbia Goodloe) / Mentions in letter / brothers Tommie / (Thomas), French, / Bobbie (Robert), sister / Mary – children / of Newton Goodloe, / the brother of Mary / Douglas & Cynthia Orr / Written from / Russellville, Ala. / June 21, 1874 |
| 289 | relationship | Fannie; Uncle Dr Clarke; Uncle John; Brother French; Russell; Eugene; Broth Tommie; little N L; Broth Bobbie; Sis Mary; Pa | Fannie keeps house for Uncle Dr Clarke (widower, 5 children); Uncle John; will live with Brother French; Tommie's children incl. 'little N L'; Bobbie in the drug store; Sis Mary weighs 84 | June 21st 1874 | 1874-06-21 | 06-027-P01; 06-028-P01 | I am now staying with my Uncle Dr Clarke he has very recently lost his wife and he has 5 little Children ... Uncle John is very anxious for me to live with him ... dear Brother French has always done so much for me ... he has two Beautiful Boys Russell and Eugene Broth Tommie has 2 [[illegible]] and little N L Broth Bobbie is with Broth French in the Drug store Sis Mary lives 2 miles from here her health is very bad now ... only weighs 84 I weigh 125 |
| 290 | event | Fannie's Pa (Newton Goodloe per slip) | Pa is in Texas and pleased; hoped he will stay with Lizzie's family or Aunt Cynthia | June 1874 | 1874-06 | 06-028-P01; 06-029-P01 | I am glad Pa is so much pleased with Texas I hope he can get business out there I wish Brother would move out there too I hope Pa will stay with you all or Aunt Cynthia one ... Tell Pa I am anxiously awaiting an answer to my letter ... Your Unknown but loving Cousin / Fannie |
| 291 | relationship | Lizzie Graves; Lizzie Douglas | Slips call Lizzie Graves 'Aunt' and 'Miss'; one slip 'Written by Miss Lizzie Graves To Aunt Lizzie' |  |  | 06-030-P01; 06-033-P01; 06-033-P02; 06-036-P01 | To Lizzie Douglas ? / from / Lizzie Graves ... Written by Miss Lizzie Graves / To Aunt Lizzie ... To Lizzie Douglas / from Aunt / [Miss Lizzie Graves] / Pratties Ala. |
| 292 | event | Lizzie Graves; Bud Joe; Bud Davy; Charlie; Pa | Bud Joe to Mobile hoping to join an emigrating company for Brazil; Pa planted ~50 acres corn; three hundred acres to cultivate; Snowden |  |  | 06-032-P01; 06-031-P01 | Pa and Bud Joe went to Snowden to day (about fifteen miles from here) ... there is an emigrating company to start from there to Brazil between the 15th & 20th of this Month he wants to get with them if he doesnt succeed in that he may land in Texas ... Bud Davy has gotten shut of the chills ... I dont know how much corn Pa has planted about fifty acres I reckon ... We have / three hundred acres to cultivate |
| 293 | person | Parson Bone; Mr. Rouse; Ellen Douglas; Sue Roberts; Mollie | Valentines and 'Flora'; Ellen Douglas has Flora |  |  | 06-031-P02; 06-031-P03 | I suppose you could not afford to go and let Parson Bone make jewely of you ... Ellen Douglas have Flora but I dont know that the emblems are all the same ... I wrote to Sue Roberts not long ago ... I wrote to Mollie not long ago |
| 294 | person | Grand ma; Bud John; Aunt Jane (Jenny's own mother); Uncle J. | Aunt Jane's family to move in; her husband and two daughters to California | Saturday 19th |  | 06-034-P01 | Aunt Jane and her family (Jennys own mother) expect to move out in a few weeks. her husband and two of her daughters and their families have gone to California Uncle J. has only gone to look at the country |
| 295 | event | Lizzie Graves | New saddle, gloves, pony that kneels; Pa's eyes worse |  |  | 06-035-P01 | I have a new saddle and new gloves and a little pony that will kneel for me to get on ... Pas eyes are getting worse |
| 296 | place | Lizzie Graves | Pratville Ala.; home named Mosshead or Buttonwood Hall | March 10 67 | 1867-03-10 | 06-037-P01; 06-038-P02; 06-036-P01 | Pratville Ala. / Sunday evening March 10 67 ... The name of our present home is Mosshead or Button wood hall |
| 297 | event | Lizzie Graves; Laura | Laura going away so far; regards to Lizzie's father and Mother and Mr [[Orr?]] | March 1867 | 1867-03 | 06-037-P02 | I am sorry for you that Laura is going away so far from you remember me very kindly to her when you see her my kindest regards your father and Mother also to Mr [[Orr?]] |
| 298 | event | Lizzie Graves | Would rather live in Texas; Pa moved to Alabama for Grand Ma and schooling; cannot go to school; sewing | March 1867 | 1867-03 | 06-038-P01 | it is true I would much rather live in Texas and I sometimes feel right sad when I think about having left there ... Grand Ma living here alone She has only one other Son ... thinking as he did that the opportunities for schooling [[inserted: us]] would be greater than to have stayed there |
| 299 | event | Cousin [[M. W.?]]; Mrs. G's little girl | Three corpses at one sight; Cousin M. W. left eight children, six girls, oldest boy about sixteen gone to Virginia | about a month ago [Aug 1868] | 1868-08 | 06-041-P01; 06-042-P01 | About a month ago I saw something that I never witnessed before—three corpses at one sight all to be laid in one family grave yard Cousin [[M. W.?]] and her little infant were laid in one coffin and Mrs. G's little girl in another ... Cousin M. left [[inserted: eight]] children—[[two?]] grown live / less, six girls, the oldest in her fifteenth year The oldest boy (about sixteen) left home a few days before her death for Virginia to go to school |
| 300 | church | Lizzie Graves; Miss Mattie B.; Mr. C. | Sunday School class of seven boys; gave a boy a Bible; lessons historical, Abraham to David | Sept 1868 | 1868-09 | 06-042-P01; 06-043-P01 | I have a class in Sunday School composed of seven boys ... he said they did not have a Bible in the family so I gave him a Bible ... We dont use any book in the class but the Bible the lessons we teach now are all historical |
| 301 | person | Annie Roberts; Frank; Bud Davy; Bud Joe | Chills; Frank sick; Bud Joe overseer four miles off | Sept 30th 1867 | 1867-09-30 | 06-045-P01; 06-046-P01 | you and Annie Roberts have been more punctual than any of my correspondents ... Frank and I both took chills ... Bud Joe has had one & I dont know he is not at home now he is living about four miles from here hiring as overseer for one of our Cousins until the cotton crop is gathered |
| 302 | event | Lizzie Graves | Fruit in Alabama: no peaches; Grand Ma's orchard destroyed by a tornado; pomegranate trees | Sept 1867 | 1867-09 | 06-046-P01 | I do want some good peaches so bad haven't had any here since I left home ... she once had an apple and pear orchard [[illegible]] it was destroyed a few years ago by a tornado that blew her house down ... Grand Ma has plenty of pomegranate trees did you ever see any? |
| 303 | church | Lizzie Graves; Grand Ma; Pa; Frank; Episcopalians; Methodists | Kin nearly all Methodists; Pa and one sister 'gone astray'; protracted Methodist meeting suspended for election days; Frank reading the Bible | Sept 30 1867 | 1867-09-30 | 06-047-P01; 06-048-P01 | Lizzie I thought I had told you that our kin were nearly all Methodists ... Pa and one of his sisters have gone astray from the teachings of their childhood my Aunt is a member of the Episcopal Church ... the meeting is suspended untill wednesday on account of today and tomorrow being election days ... Frank has been reading the Bible a good deal lately I do wish we had a good preacher I think his mind is now open to receive the truth ... I think Grand Ma [[tries?]] hopes to win us all over to the Methodist church |
| 304 | event | Lizzie Graves; Mr. — ('my beau') | Evening ride to a persimmon tree; 'he doesn't suit my style' | Sept 30 1867 | 1867-09-30 | 06-048-P02 | Mr. — ("my beau") was there, he asked me to take a ride with [[inserted: him]] and Oh! we had a "[[time of it?]]!" rode on horse back we went to a persimmon tree ... he is a very nice young man but he doesn't "suit my style" so much of my nonsense |
| 305 | place | Pa (Graves); Montgomery; Lowndes | Pa to Montgomery and Lowndes; crops tolerably good | Sept 1867 | 1867-09 | 06-048-P03 | Crops are tolerably good here corn is turning out pretty well Cotton better than I expected ... Pa went to Montgomery [[deleted: illegible]] [[inserted: to day]] Tuesday ... he expects to go to Lowndes tomorrow |
| 306 | event | Lizzie Graves | Sick in bed nearly a week; 'an Ala. girl' (pale faces) | Aug. 29th 1867 | 1867-08-29 | 06-050-P01 | My sickness has served to make me look like an Ala. girl the first morning that I was able to fill my seat at the table some of them said "Cousin Lizzie your sickness shows right smartly on you" I said "Oh! it is only helping to make an Ala. girl of me" ... "we dont have as many pale faces there as you do here" |
| 307 | church | Lizzie Graves; Pa; Mr. Thurgeson; Christian Church; Liberty | Heard of a Christian church twelve or fifteen miles south; Pa and she 'alone'; 'series of doctrinal sermons like they do at Liberty' | Aug 1867 | 1867-08 | 06-051-P02; 06-050-P02 | Pa heard of a christian Church some twelve or fifteen miles south of this ... I havnt met up with any one yet who is a member of the Christian Church Pa and I are alone it makes me feel right badly ... we dont have series of doctrinal sermons like they do at Liberty |
| 308 | person | Mr. Ba; Mr. Horns; Mr. Goodman; Mr. Austin; Jane and Mary; Aunt Susanna Andrews (Graves) | Examinations; Mr. Goodman moving; letter enclosed for Jane and Mary ('the boys'); storm reported by Aunt Susanna Andrews (Graves) | Aug 1867 | 1867-08 | 06-051-P01; 06-050-P02; 06-051-P03 | I wish very much I could have been with you at Mr. Ba's examination ... Lizzie do you ever hear anything from Mr. Goodman these days poor man I reckon he will move and move until he settle in his grave ... Where is Mr. Austin ... I have written a letter to Jane and Mary which I hope you will pardon me for sending in yours give it to any of or all the boys and they will get some person to read it for them ... I received a letter from Aunt Susanna Andrews (Graves) a few days ago |
| 309 | relationship | Lizzie Graves; Dr. (L. Douglas) & Ellen | Slip: May 10 1867, regards given to Dr. (L. Douglas) & Ellen; letter gives regards 'to the Dr. and Ellen also to Maggy and Jennie Bell' | May 10, 1867 | 1867-05-10 | 06-052-P01; 06-054-P01 | (Regards given to Dr. (L. Douglas) / & Ellen. ... Give my kind regards to the Dr. and Ellen also to Maggy and Jennie Bell and to all enquiring friends ... L. J. G. |
| 310 | event | Lizzie Graves | Pa's sore eyes; chills broken with red pepper; brothers Frank, [[Thaddie?]] & Charles at work | May 10th 1867 | 1867-05-10 | 06-053-P01; 06-054-P01 | he has had sore eyes very bad but they are improving now I very much feared he would entirely lose one of them ... had two or three chills but broke them up with red pepper / I have been quite well since then Brothers are now at work trying to earn their bread by the sweat of their brow work seems to agree with Frank [[Thaddie?]] & Charles |
| 311 | event | Memorial decorations, Alabama 1867 | May party at Tusk[egee?] to decorate Confederate graves; the colored ladies of Montgomery decorated the Yankee dead; Methodist reunion convention; Catholics | May 1867 | 1867-05 | 06-054-P01; 06-054-P02 | There was a May party given by the ladies at Tusk[[illegible]] the first of this month ... to decorate the graves of the Alabama confederate dead A few days after the white ladies had enjoyed their [[deleted: illegible]] on the confederate dead The colored ladies of the city of Montgomery formed a procession marched down to the graveyard loaded with flowers to decorate the graves of the Yankee dead ... The church convention is now in [[illegible]] for the purpose of uniting the Methodist Episcopal and Methodist Protestant churches again ... The Catholics have been making quite a [[illegible]] here lately |
| 312 | money | Corn price | Corn selling at $2.00 per bushel (Alabama, May 1867) | May 1867 | 1867-05 | 06-054-P02 | he is going to carry corn off for sale it is selling at $2.00 per bushel |
| 313 | event | Lizzie Graves; Uncle J.; Aunt; Cousin Mary | Uncle returned from California delighted; cousins moved to Los Angeles; earthquakes; bought a home in Greenville thirty miles off; beaus | Nov. 20th 1868 | 1868-11-20 | 06-056-P02; 06-057-P01; 06-057-P02 | My Uncle has returned from California highly delighted with every thing ... Sanfrancisco is far a head of Montgomery in the fashions if not ahead of New York ... My cousins found it too gay for them, so they moved about 500 five hundred miles south of that to Los angeles ... I think the Earth quakes they have been having out there have shaken his determination ... he has bought a home in Greenville, about thirty miles from here ... We have been having a good many "beaus" since Cousin M. came |
| 314 | event | Mr Winniford's marriage | Lizzie Graves has not heard of Mr Winnifords marriage; sympathizes with Lizzie's loneliness | Nov 1868 | 1868-11 | 06-056-P02 | I have not heard of Mr Winnifords marriage I know how to sympathize with you in your loneliness |
| 315 | relationship | M E Goodloe; Lizzie Douglas | Slip: to Lizzie Douglas from (Niece) M E Goodloe, Randolph (Field); letter addresses Lizzie as 'Dear Niece' | Feb 12, 1865 | 1865-02-12 | 06-059-P01; 06-060-P01 | To Lizzie Douglas / from (Niece) / M E Goodloe / Randolph – (Field) / Feb 12, 1865 ... Randolph Feb the 12 1865 / Miss Lizzie D. / Dear Niece |
| 316 | event | Henry Orr | Passing through on his way to his command two days' ride away; five weeks from his command, detained in Louisiana with a lame horse | Feb 1865 | 1865-02 | 06-060-P01 | I was allso agreable surprised in a viset from Henry Orr the other day he was passing thru this Co on his way to his command which is in two days ride of this plaise he only staid too nights with us he was well He has not ben withe the command in 5 weeks he was ditained in Luisiana on the account of his horse being lame |
| 317 | person | Uncle Tom; Granvill and Babe; Nannie; Mary Orr; Laura; Sue | M E Goodloe's kin; party with supper and dance; 'my brittest hopes have been blasted' | Feb 1865 | 1865-02 | 06-061-P01; 06-061-P02 | I was at Uncle Toms when he came we wer just starting to a party ... I did justice to the Supper but of cours tuck no hands in the dance ... Lizzie I do not think their is any more purfict happiness in this world for me for my brittest hopes have been blasted ... I heard from Granvill and Babe the other day ... Nannie is bothering me ... Tell Mary Orr that I think it time that she had answered my letter tell Laura ... tell Sue to write to me |
| 318 | event | M E Goodloe; George Douglas | Received a letter from George Douglas and answered it; 'old Houston' | Feb 1865 | 1865-02 | 06-061-P02 | I received a letter from George Douglas and answered it and think it time that I was getting a nother one / Lizzie their is some very pretty girls and boys in old Houston |
| 319 | event | Mrs [[Stinnet?]] (Claunch) murder | Sisters' account: Mrs [[S?]] killed; daughter Mollie aged 15 suspected; girl's bloody dress found; bond of [[five?]] hundred dollars; jailed at [[Wax?]]; Mrs Douglas first on the scene | [Sept 1863] | 1863-09 | 06-063-P01 | I have a horrible tale to recount this week tis the murder of Mrs [[Stinnet?]] by her daughter. Mrs [[S?]] is a sister to Mr [[Simons?]] ... Mrs Douglas was the first one to get there after it happened, and [[illegible]] poor crying little child & asked it who kilt her Mother – [[illegible]] the child said it was Mollie. Search was made and the girls dress was found concealed, all bloody. ... three days was given her to give [[five?]] hundred dollars bond she could not so her [[illegible]] went to [[Wax?]] jail. |
| 320 | person | Sister; Pleas Tyler; John; [[Morriss?]]; Mr [[Morton?]] | Laura's Friday Sept 18 page; Sister's children | Friday Sept 18 |  | 06-064-P01 | Sisters children came home with me this morning she came this evening They are all well and hearty Sister is quite uneasy about her [[beef?]] ... Pleas Tyler is [[illegible]] and I heard him say he would be gone about ten days |
| 321 | money | Lizzie (to George) | Bought a saddle for twelve dollars; packing something for 'you boys' | Saturday evening 20th |  | 06-064-P02 | I have got me a beautiful saddle got it [[illegible]] Mrs [[illegible]] brought it [[illegible]] twelve dollars |
| 322 | event | Magruder; flour speculation | Wheat carried in before the tithe tax sold by speculators; man from Nacochitoches says Magruder is at the head of it; sending George a sack of flour and fat bacon for his fish | [1863-64] |  | 06-066-P01 | It is now currently reported and believed, that the wheat which the Farmers carried in before the tythe tax, was due, in order that the Soldiers might get some flour has been sold by speculators. ... There was a man here the other day from Nacochitoches who says this is so, and that Magruder is at the head of it. ... What if old Magruder orders you away from Sabine about the time Joda gets back, and takes all of your flour and bacon away from you? We are going to send you a sack of flour a piece and a good supply of fat bacon which I hope will serve to make your fish a little more savory. |
| 323 | person | Joda; Mr Banks; Col. [[illegible]] | Joda saw Mr Banks in Dallas; bridle cannot be got; blanket mended |  |  | 06-067-P01 | Joda saw Mr Banks in Dallas the last day he was there ... We will send all the things required, without it is the bridle. ... I have finished mending your blanket. |
| 324 | event | Joda; Velasco; Dock; J. M.; Mr Bell | Joda arrived 18 April 1865; George returned from Velasco; thread sent by J. M.; flour speculation (600 lbs) to send by Joda; side saddle for Laura from Houston | April 21st 1865 | 1865-04-21 | 06-069-P02 | We were somewhat surprised last tuesday evening — the 18th by the arrival of Joda ... suppose you have written by mail since your return from Velasco. I am anxious to hear something about Dock ... The thread you sent by J. M. was very acceptable, it will save me many a turn of my "Piano." ... It is to send down some flour by Joda that Laura and I have. There is only six hundred pounds of it ... I would like for you to look around a little, and see if a side saddle could be got in Houston. Laura is very anxious to get one |
| 325 | event | Laura | Teaching school away from home, 16 to 19 scholars | April 1865 | 1865-04 | 06-070-P01 | I recd a letter from Laura this evening. she seems to be very well satisfied in her new situation. says she has from 16 to 19 schollars, with a prospect for more. I suppose it is better than to be carding and spinning |
| 326 | person | Cousin Mollie; Mrs. Bishop; May; Kirkham | Cousin Mollie at school at Liberty; Mrs. Bishop from Dallas teaching there | April 22 1865 | 1865-04-22 | 06-070-P02 | She is going to Liberty; A mrs. Bishop is teaching there now. She is from Dallas ... could teach almost every thing the Dead Languages included. But May says it is with difficulty she can find the answers in old Kirkham |
| 327 | event | Stiles boys; Stanley; Jim Floyd; Mrs Campbell; Jennie Bell | Horse theft; deserter Stiles boys; one hung on the limb Stanley was hung on; party of 8 or 10 men; 'I dread the consequences of this mob law' | April 1865 | 1865-04 | 06-070-P02; 06-071-P01 | Several horses have been stolen, and two Stiles boys who lived over in the neck and were deserters, were suspected. A company of men got after them, and caught one of them — the Youngest; he acknowledged to stealing and said his brother was concerned in it. They hung him on the same limb Stanley was hung on — so we have heard. ... Last Sunday morning a party of 8 or 10 men passed Mrs Campbells — aunt [[Bats?]] house — with one of the Stileses. Jim Floyd was one of the party. ... Perhaps they merited death, but I dread the consequences of this mob law. We know not, but this is the beginning of terrible times. I hate the thought that Brushy should be the place of the beginning of such things. |
| 328 | person | [[Foythe?]] (Fayette); Bob | Fayette not returned; Bob chills, seeking furlough | April 1865 | 1865-04 | 06-071-P02 | [[Foythe?]] has not yet returned; the last news from them, Bob was having the chills, and was trying to get a furlough to come with F. |
| 329 | event | Henry and Ellen; Father | Henry and Ellen went up (home) last Wednesday; Henry helped Father sow grass seed; stove set up; well cleaned | April 1865 | 1865-04 | 06-072-P01 | Henry and Ellen went up last wednesday, so we are once more by our selves. Henry helped Father to sow his grass seed before he went away. They have got the stove set up ... They got the top of the well, a good many old shoes and large rocks and a Young possum out of the well. |
| 330 | church | brother Methodist Jones; Liberty | Will hold forth at Liberty; Lizzie has not heard a discourse since 1 March | April 1865 | 1865-04 | 06-072-P02 | Tomorrow, brother Methodist Jones, will hold forth at Liberty. I will try to attend as I have not heard a discourse since the 1st of March. |
| 331 | person | Joda; [[Fran?]]; Warren | Joda at home plowing; two letters from [[Fran?]] dated 26 March; love to Warren | April 1865 | 1865-04 | 06-072-P03; 06-072-P04 | Joda says tell you he is at home safe, and a plowing. ... We recd two letters from [[Fran?]], last dated 26th of March. He is very anxious to come home ... With best love to Warren and yourself I remain Lizzie |
| 332 | person | Jeppe; Mr Rutherford; Mary Burkhead; Theodore Wylie; Miss Mattie; Sis | Jeppe bought Laura's pony going to San Antonio; nag bought from Mr Rutherford; George's 'almost rude word' to Sis | March 20 [year not given] |  | 06-074-P01; 06-075-P02; 06-075-P03 | Jeppe bought Laura's pony when he went to San Antonio I consider him a poor horse ... You say "Sis sends her respects to me, ask her what I must do with them." ... we bought her from Mr Rutherford, father of the one that married Mary Burkhead; If you remember, Mr Rutherford bought out Theodore Wylies; and by the by he has a daughter just "Sweet Sixteen" Miss Mattie |
| 333 | event | Lizzie to George | Advice on George's handwriting and spelling; letters marked 'Due ten cents' |  |  | 06-074-P02; 06-074-P03 | I know you know how to spell, but you sometimes leave out letters, which causes [[deleted: them]] [[inserted: words]] to be spelt wrong. ... your letters are sometimes marked "Due ten cents". |
| 334 | event | Mr Graves; Thos; Joe | Joe taken prisoner and paroled by the Feds | March 20th |  | 06-074-P05 | March 20th all well today Mr Graves Thos Uncle came over to work this morning they say Joe has got home he was taken prisner and Paroled by the Feds. |
| 335 | person | Mr. Graves; Dolpheus; Laura | Laura's fragment: Mr. Graves planting corn; the boy whose hand was mashed off | Thursday 28 [Apr.] |  | 06-077-P01; 06-078-P01; 06-078-P02 | Thursday 28. Mr. Graves commenced planting corn ... I did not send one by Dolpheus / In your next give us the name of the Boy that got his hand mashed off. ... Affectionately / Laura |
| 336 | event | Battle below Shreveport | Fragment: fight below Shreveport, two thousand prisoners; slip says Reb capture of 2000 prisoners | 17th |  | 06-079-P01; 06-080-P01 | — Tells of battle / below Shreveport — / with Reb capture of / 2000 prisoners ... 17th We [[illegible]] just [[illegible]] us [[illegible]] fight below Shreveport. [[illegible]] says we [[illegible]] two thousand prisoners, five [[illegible]]. [[deleted: cannon]] [[illegible]] forty |
| 337 | church | Sunday school | Sunday school commences today; 'all well on Brushy' |  |  | 06-081-P02 | Sunday school commences to day I dont expect to attend much. This leaves all well on Brushy. |
| 338 | event | Laura (per slip) to George | Slip: Feb. 11, 1865(?); letter dated Feb 1st 1865 'By the fire Sandy'; run on Blazes leading Jenney; spinning; Lizzie & Jeannie deserted us | Feb 1st 1865 | 1865-02-01 | 06-082-P01; 06-083-P01; 06-083-P02; 06-084-P01 | By the fire Sandy / [[illegible]] Feb 1st / 1865 / Dear George ... I had quite a run on Blazes leading Jenney ... Spinning is my constant employmen at home ... Lizzie & Jeannie have entire deserted us ... Keep out of mischief dont forget [[how Lol?]] |
| 339 | event | Lizzie to George, letter No. 8 | Brushy, Thursday night April 14 1864; the Dr.; all well on Hack except Joe; Jeppe coming down; Beck and [[Wash?]] from Grape Vine Springs because water is scarce; Ellen wants to [[sell?]] out and move to this neighborhood; militia service ten days out of forty; deserters at [[Tyler?]]; Mrs White presented a six shooter | April 14th 1864 | 1864-04-14 | 06-086-P01; 06-086-P02 | No. 8 / Brushy Texas / Thursday night April 14th 1864 / Dear brother — ... They were all well on Hack except Joe ... Jeppe will be coming down [[illegible]] here now. Beck and [[Wash?]] have had to come [[illegible]] from Grape Vine Springs. On account of water being so scarce ... They only have to [[illegible]] ten days out of every forty. He thinks if he [[illegible]] Ellen he will be exempt on account of [[illegible]] militia. ... Mrs White presented a six shooter and told them if they advanced she would shoot them. so they did not take Mr White. Hurrah! for some of the women dont you say so. |
| 340 | event | Joda; Watts; Ingeville; Johnson; John Turney | Turkey hunting; pet deer; panther; six mule team | 15th [April 1864] | 1864-04-15 | 06-087-P01 | [[Colburn?]] came to stay all night to go a turkey hunting with Joda in the morning, but was disappointed as Joda went to Dallas to day to see Watts having failed to see him at Ingeville as he went to Johnson ... You ought to be here to hear the turkeys gobble of a morning and see some of our beautiful pet deer. ... which [[illegible]] we suppose to be a panther. ... On the subject of John Turneys, the people of [[Tennessee?]] are anxious that he shall get a six mule team |
| 341 | event | Genl McCullough; William Duff; Jno Smith; Rome | Peace questions; the cotton trade; McCullough's rendezvous trap capturing 200 men; Rome to Wax. seeking to drive a team San Antonio to Eagle Pass | April 15 1864 | 1864-04-15 | 06-087-P02 | You say dont get too fast on the peace questions I will not, for I never was as sanguine as Father and [[Louisa?]] ... But I always have and always will think there is something wrong about this cotton trade. ... Genl McCullough ... two hundred of them had arrived when they were surrounded by a Regmt and taken prisoners ... [[deleted: among them William Duff and his]] nephews Jno Smith who have returned to this section ... Rome has now gone to Wax. with a waggon master ... to try to get to drive a team from San Antonio to Eagle Pass. |
| 342 | relationship | Lizzie Douglas; George Douglas | Slip: Nov. 13, 1864; mentions Henry & Ellen's safe return, prisoners at Tyler, plastic surgery | Nov. 13, 1864 | 1864-11-13 | 06-088-P01 | To George Douglas / from / Lizzie Douglas / Nov. 13, 1864 / — / mentions Henry & / Ellen Douglas safe / return. / Speaks of prisoners at / Tyler, Tex. / Tells of plastic surgery |
| 343 | church | Liberty; Mrs Justin's funeral; Haynes, Justin, Maley, White, Berry | Funeral sermon at Liberty from Hebrews 11:4, two hours, much on depreciation of the currency; the Colony; Shilo | Nov. 13th 1864 | 1864-11-13 | 06-089-P02 | This morning Lou and I went to meeting to Liberty to hear Mrs Justins funeral preached; it was to have been preached at the Colony last Sunday ... Five preachers: Haynes, Justin, Maley, White, and Berry. Mr Haynes gave us a long tedious discourse from the last clause of the 4th vs. of the 11th ch. Hebrews. He spoke fully two hours, and a good portion of his sermon was about the depreciation of the currency |
| 344 | event | Aunt Bettie; Betsy | Aunt Bettie moved (to Arkansas) on the 10th [Nov 1864] taking Betsy and chickens | Nov 10 1864 | 1864-11-10 | 06-089-P02; 06-090-P01; 06-092-P04 | We assisted aunt Bettie in getting ready to move ... She got off on the morning of the 10th / She took Betsy and chickens, left the spinnack and what few cows she had ... Aunt Bettie told me to tell you to write to her [[illegible]] Ark. |
| 345 | money | Father (N. L. Douglas) | Bought a sow and five shoats for ten dollars specie; reserved twenty acres for corn and hungarian; Gibson to raise wheat for a fourth | Nov 1864 | 1864-11 | 06-090-P01 | Father bought a sow and five shoats from her for ten dollars specie, so I reckon we are safe in the meat line for another year ... Father has reserved twenty acres for [[corn?]] and hungarian. ... he was just to raise the wheat, cut it and thrash it and put a fourth in the granary. |
| 346 | event | The well | Well waters a dozen mules and horses | Nov 1864 | 1864-11 | 06-090-P01 | The well holds out finely about a dozen mules and horses are watered out of it twice and three times a day. |
| 347 | event | Old Abe; [[Childsy?]]; Mc. Clelland | Lincoln's election; four more years of war feared | Nov 1864 | 1864-11 | 06-090-P01 | If I had any hopes of the war ending soon I would feel so much better but from the tone of the papers, we need not look for peace in four more years. Old Abe is elected in all probability and [[Childsy?]] thinks it is best for if Mc. Clelland is elected says he, propositions will be made which some of the States will accept, and then we are ruined. |
| 348 | person | [[Childsy?]]; Mr Grounds | Soldier visitors from George's camp | Nov 1864 | 1864-11 | 06-090-P02; 06-091-P01 | [[Childsy?]] was nearly frozen, but a warm fire soon set him to talking, and he and Mr Grounds (a soldier that came with him) kept us laughing ... you and Joda had talked so much about him |
| 349 | event | Henry and Ellen; Warren; Beck; Griffin | Henry and Ellen started home 8 Nov 1864; Warren expected with Beck; 'Griffin or somebody else must have a particular spite at Warren' | 8th [Nov 1864] | 1864-11-08 | 06-091-P02 | Henry and Ellen started home on the 8th, all well and hearty. we have not heard from Warren but look for him and Beck down. Griffin or somebody else must have a particular spite at Warren. If he did not get his furlough lengthened, his stay at home will be short. |
| 350 | unit | Dock [[Wyrman?]]; Ed Dickson | Dock in Co. G, Bates' Reg at Velasco; 'I am a peace man, let it come as it may'; Ed Dickson at Tyler guarding prisoners | 10th Oct 1864 | 1864-10-10 | 06-091-P02 | We recd a letter from Dock [[Wyrman?]] not long since, dated 10th Oct. He had just got Fathers letter and the socks we sent by Ed Dickson. ... He belongs to Co. G. Bates' Reg which is stationed at Velasco ... He winds up his letter by saying "I am a peace man, let it come as it may." Ed Dickson is now at Tyler guarding prisoners. |
| 351 | event | [[Rome?]]; Sister | Rome tells Sister to buy two more wagons and come to San Antonio in the spring | Nov 1864 | 1864-11 | 06-091-P02 | [[Rome?]] it seems has a notion of hanging on to the service. The last letter Sister got from him he told her to buy two more waggons (a horse and an ox waggon) and come to him in the spring. Sister is mightily pleased, she has been wanting to leave here a long time. She thinks she can rent a house in San Antonio and send the children to school |
| 352 | event | Jeppe; Warren; Beck | Jeppe arrived 15 Nov 1864, going to San Antonio with wool; Warren cannot bring Beck; will be here 10 Dec on his way back to Sabine | Wednesday 16th [Nov 1864] | 1864-11-16 | 06-092-P01 | Jeppe got here yesterday he will only stay down a few days, as he is going to San Antonio with a load of wool as soon as he returns. Henry and Ellen [[inserted: had]] got back safe. Warren not got down; he can get no conveyance to bring Beck down while he stays; he wrote he would be here on the 10th of Dec. on his way back to Sabine. I suppose he got his furlough lengthened. |
| 353 | event | Mr. Marshall's family; Lou's house | Family from Arkansas, two sons and six daughters; 'the feds treated them better than our folks' | Nov 1864 | 1864-11 | 06-092-P01 | On last monday Mr. Marshalls family [[deleted: was]] rolled in from Ark. composed of two sons and six daughters. ... The jayHawkers were the only ones that pestered them much. They say the feds treated them better than our folks. The old man has rented Lous house for them |
| 354 | event | [[Lidda?]] Baker; Dr. Mc. [[Curdy?]] | Gold nose covered with forehead skin | Nov 1864 | 1864-11 | 06-092-P02 | As an other item of news I will tell you about [[Lidda?]] Bakers nose. Dr. Mc. [[Curdy?]] is making her a new one; he made a gold nose and then cut a piece of skin out of her forehead and twisted it around and covered the gold. ... He says he is going to make her a lip when her nose gets well and then take her for pay. |
| 355 | event | Jane Green | Died of pneumonia | Nov 1864 | 1864-11 | 06-092-P03 | Jane Green died last Saturday night with pneumonia. |
| 356 | relationship | Lizzie Douglas; George Douglas | Slip: Feb. 19, 1864; mentions Jim Orr's letter, wounded at Chickamauga; deserters; Quantrill's Raiders | Feb. 19, 1864 | 1864-02-19 | 06-093-P01 | To George Douglas / from Sister / Lizzie Douglas / Brushy — / Feb. 19, 1864 / [[deleted: Letter no. 4]] / mentions Jim Orr's / letter — wounded at / Chickamauga. / describes war & / Lizzie also speaks of / deserters — / Quantrills Raiders |
| 357 | event | Fayette; M Orr; Jim; Tole Hamilton | Letters from Fayette and Jim; Jim slightly wounded at Chickamauga; Tole Hamilton fell at Ringold | Feb. 19th 1864 | 1864-02-19 | 06-094-P02; 06-094-P03 | Laura got a letter from Fayette, M Orr one from Fayette, Mary and I one apiece from Jim. / They have come safely through all the fights except the slight wound Jim rec. at Chickamauga. ... Jim says "he can stand the fatigue and hardships all very well, till it comes to the fighting fighting, and then when he sees his comrades falling around him, it is all he can do to persuade himself not to run." Tole Hamilton fell at Ringold, in the retreat. he was shot through the breast, and only lived a few minutes. ... He leaves a widow and child. |
| 358 | event | Goodloes (Aquilla's sons); Squire; uncle [[Givans?]]; Charly Brown; Will; Will G.; Will Brown; Mary G.; Billy | Two Goodloes deserted; Squire 'safe over the Border'; Will safe in a Northern clime | Feb 1864 | 1864-02 | 06-094-P03; 06-095-P01 | The boys write home that two Goodloes (Aquilla's sons) have deserted. And whose name do you expect to hear [[illegible]]? Tell no other than Squire. ... But Squire is safe over the Border, where a many another poor Dixie boy wishes he was I expect. ... Charly Brown has written to them, that Will is safe with his kindred in a Northern Clime ... Mary G. is perfectly satisfied about Billy. ... He was at Chicago when Charly heard from him |
| 359 | unit | George's battalion; Joda; Tom [[Warthen?]]; Gurley's reg.; Murray's Texas Co. | Joda thinks George's battalion disgraced; guard round Gurley's regiment; Murray's Texas Co. all left but sixteen | Feb 1864 | 1864-02 | 06-094-P03 | From the tone of Jodas letter, he seems to think your Batt. is disgraced worse than any other body of men in the Army, but he is behind the times ... Tom [[Warthen?]] writes home that they have to keep a guard round Gurleys reg. to keep them from deserting. We hear there all of Murrays Texas Co. have left but Sixteen. |
| 360 | event | Quantrill's men; [[Sherrol?]] | Men in the Confederacy who murder for money; Sherrol's tales of killing union men | Feb 1864 | 1864-02 | 06-095-P02 | I think there are men in this Confederacy, mean enough to perpetrate any and all of the crimes committed by him, and that for less motive, than Quantrills men were actuated by. ... But there are men in this confederacy who murder for money and I have some reason to believe, that [[Sherrol?]] is one of them. ... Such as going to a union man's house, passing himself off for a union man get into the others Sentiments, then draw out his pistol and shoot him down. |
| 361 | event | Jim White; militia | Militia armed with sticks | Saturday night 20 [Feb 1864] | 1864-02-20 | 06-095-P03 | Jim White says the militia [[illegible]] are armed with sticks. ... he says his command had guns as they were dismounted Cavalry, but some of them were actually armed with sticks for guns. |
| 362 | person | Emmie; uncle Geo.; Cousin Mollie; Laura; baby May; Ellen | Emmie asks uncle Geo. for little letters; George's poems 'We are growing old' and 'O would I were a child again'; the baby, blue-eyed little May | Feb 1864 | 1864-02 | 06-095-P04 | I asked Emmie what to tell uncle Geo. she says tell he do write me little letters. Cousin Mollie is here to night. ... Laura wishes to know if you completed "We are growing old" and "O would I were a child again" they are beautiful verses. I never once told you a word about the baby. She is a very pretty, blue eyed fair skinned little May. ... Ellen and Emmie have grown a great deal since you left. Ellen can spin pretty well on two spindles. |
| 363 | relationship | Lizzie; George; Lafayette Orr; Henry Orr; Cynthia; Jim Orr; Uncle Alfred | Slip: June 28, 1864, Letter no. 18: Lafayette Orr prisoner of war, through Henry Orr's letter to Cynthia & Jim Orr; death of Uncle Alfred (manuscript numbered No. 13) | June 28, 1864 | 1864-06-28 | 06-096-P01; 06-097-P01 | — Tells of hearing from / Lafayette Orr as / prisoner of war thru / Henry Orr letter to / Cynthia & Jim Orr / — Tells of death of / Uncle Alfred ... No. 13. / Brushy, June 28th 1864. |
| 364 | event | Uncle Alfred; Aunt Bettie; Mr Orr; Henry | Uncle Alfred died late Sunday evening 5 June 1864, 18 days after being wounded; Aunt Bettie fainted; Mary Orr and Lizzie sat with her | 5th of this month [June 1864] | 1864-06-05 | 06-097-P02 | I have sorrowful news to tell you this morning. Uncle Alfred is dead. Mr Orr recd a letter from Henry yesterday, confirming what we had heard before. He died late Sunday evening the 5th of this month, just 18 days after he was wounded. Poor Aunt Bettie is allmost crushed beneath the blow. As soon as she heard it was really true, she fainted away |
| 365 | event | Uncle Alfred's wound | Bone fractured below the knee cap | June 1864 | 1864-06 | 06-097-P03 | the bone had been considerably fractured, just below the knee cap, and upon pressing it a good deal of matter run out with the oil of the knee cap. |
| 366 | event | Uncle Alf; John Morgan; Docter Gardner; Bob [[Head?]]; cousin Gran; Bob; Thos. Harper; Parsons men; Coz G | 'I just want to get into one big fight, that will do me'; buried between John Morgan and Docter Gardner; Bob says he was the coolest man in the company; Parsons' men to Houston | June 1864 | 1864-06 | 06-098-P01 | Poor Uncle Alf. when he told Lou and me goodbye he said "I just want to get into one big fight, that will do me." But now he sleeps between two comrades in arms — John Morgan, and [[deleted: illegible]] [[inserted: Docter]] Gardner. Bob [[Head?]] and his lady called upon us ... He says Uncle A. was a good soldier — he watched him during the fight and he appeared to be the coolest man in the Co. Henry writes that cousin Gran is able to walk some ... Thos. Harper got in the other day, he says Parsons men have been ordered to Houston. |
| 367 | church | Methodists at Liberty; Parson Sansom; [[Coltr V.?]] | Methodist meeting at Liberty, seats moved under the trees; dinner on the ground | June 1864 | 1864-06 | 06-098-P02 | Well the Methodists are having another grand time at Liberty; their meeting commenced last Thursday night ... they had to move the seats out under the trees. ... Parson Sansom preached in the morning, [[Coltr V.?]] in the evening. Had a splendid dinner on the ground and pretty girls were plenty |
| 368 | event | Old Mrs [[Liz?]] Hawkins; Old Mr Love; Old man Durst; widow Smith of Johnson Co; Sweet; Jim | Marriages; widow Smith reported to have a hundred negroes; Jim reported gone to the Feds | June 1864 | 1864-06 | 06-098-P03 | Last Sunday night Old Mrs [[Liz?]] Hawkins and Old Mr Love were united in the bonds of matrimony; and not long since, Old man Durst and a widow Smith of Johnson Co; It is reported she has a hundred negroes. ... I saw Sweet Sunday, it is reported that Jim has gone to the Feds, but I hardly think he would marry and go right straight off and leave his wife. |
| 369 | event | Fayette; Jim; George's 24-page letter | Fayette's letters via Henry; men reenlisted 'for 50 years or during the war'; George's letter of 24 pages foolscap giving an account of himself since entering the service | June 1864 | 1864-06 | 06-098-P03; 06-098-P04; 06-099-P01 | I recd two letters from Fayette yesterday which Henry had recd and sent home. ... the most of them have reenlisted for 50 years or during the war. He says Jim is as lazy as he can be ... He said he had just recd your letter of 24 pages foolscap giving him an acct of yourself since you entered the service; that you had used up Walker and Websters besides using various / "Technical terms" |
| 370 | event | Baby Sarah; Gale; Mother; sister | Baby (Jesse's daughter) named Sarah by Gale; Mother took her to sister's because of mosquitoes | June 1864 | 1864-06 | 06-099-P02 | Mother took the little baby over to sisters nearly a week ago to stay till they disappear some. We will call the baby Sarah. I do not think it is a pretty name, but Gale named her that. |
| 371 | church | Mary Jeffers; the Colony presbyterian meeting; John Andrews; Adolphus (Delphus) | Meeting continuing; John Andrews and Adolphus home to cut wheat; the little book sent to Ellen | June 1864 | 1864-06 | 06-099-P03 | Little Mary Jeffers is the only one that has professed yet ... Big presbyterian meeting commences at the Colony to morrow. John Andrews and Adolphus are at home now got here home to cut wheat. ... We recd yours of the 9th the other day, also the little book to Ellen. |
| 372 | relationship | Sue Bell; Lizzie Douglas; George | Slip: to Brother (George) from Sue Bell & Lizzie Douglas, Aug. 19, 1864, Letter no. 16 | Aug. 19, 1864 | 1864-08-19 | 06-100-P01 | To Brother (George) / from Sue Bell & Lizzie / Douglas / Aug. 19, 1864 / Letter no. 16 |
| 373 | event | Sue (at Liberty school house); Mr. Rockett; Joda; [[W?]] Jones; Henry; Rome | Sue teaching at Liberty school house; paper sent; Rome hauling cotton below San Antonio for Laredo; promise of hauling to Matamoras | August [[18th?]] 1864 | 1864-08-18 | 06-101-P01; 06-101-P02 | Liberty school house / August [[18th?]] 1864 No 16 ... Mr. Rockett came in this morning, and handed Me a roll of paper which I suppose you must have sent ... [[Sue?]] had just recd a letter from Rome. They were four miles below SanAntonio loaded for Laredo. ... After they get back to San Antonio they have the promise of hauling to Matamoras |
| 374 | event | Sue; Lizzie Graves; Liberty Spring; Miss Beckett; Sabine fleas | Lizzie Graves the only girl of her age at school; old Liberty Spring; fleas 'as thick as flies down at Sabine'; Parson Justin's wife died | Aug 1864 | 1864-08 | 06-102-P01 | [[illegible]] and Lizzie Graves are the only girls of my age ... the old Liberty Spring. We go there [[illegible]] every day to eat our dinner ... plenty of fleas. They are about as thick as flies down at Sabine ... I heard this morning of the death of Parson Justins wife she died yesterday |
| 375 | church | Bro Goodwin; spelling match | Bro Goodwin and the boys arranged benches for a spelling match | Saturday evening [Aug 1864] | 1864-08 | 06-102-P02 | Bro Goodwin and the boys took down the stove, and moved the benches round generally ... then began the bustle of spelling. |
| 376 | relationship | Ann & Cynthia; aunt Catherine; Warren Payne; Preston Payne | Warren Payne dying, youngest child of Preston Payne, taken by aunt Catherine at nine days old; chronic diarrhoea; nearly 17 months | Aug 1864 | 1864-08 | 06-103-P01; 06-103-P02 | Ann & Cynthia have just passed by going from aunt Catherines and say Warren Payne is dying ... Warren Payne is the youngest child of Preston Payne whom aunt [[inserted: Catherine]] took when he was only nine days old ... His disease was chronic diarhea. |
| 377 | unit | Lynn Norman; Eppa; Sibley's Brigade; Gran Jetter (Jetton); Mr. Chappell; Bob; Ada Chappell; Dock | Lynn Norman home on furlough from the Rio Grande, to report at Sabinetown 8 Sept; Eppa's regiment ordered to Arizona; Gran Jetton digging a well, patriotism cooled; Chappells 'violent secesh'; Dock deserted | Aug 1864 | 1864-08 | 06-103-P02 | Lynn Norman is at home on furlough; he has been out on the RioGrande for some months but is on his way to La. He has to report at Sabinetown on the eighth of Sept. He says the Reg. to which Eppa belongs has been ordered to Arizona. Another wildgoose chase like that of Sibleys Brigade. Gran Jetter is still at home. ... His patriotism has cooled down several degrees below zero. ... Miss Ada Chappell is living at Gran Jettons ... for Dock deserted and went home from La. last fall |
| 378 | person | Ellen and Laura [[Coffy?]]; Mrs [[Graithwaite?]]; Jennie Bell; uncle Johny; Joe Witherspoon; Mr and Miss Anthony; uncle Joe; Tom Witherspoon | Sat up till two o'clock; Tom Witherspoon home with a hole in his head | August 21st [1864] | 1864-08-21 | 06-104-P01; 06-104-P02 | Ellen and Laura [[Coffy?]] Mrs [[Graithwaite?]] Jennie Bell and uncle Johny Joe Witherspoon and Mr and Miss Anthony were at uncles Joes. ... Tom Witherspoon is now at home with a hole in his head large enough to put your thumb in, the covering of his brain can be seen. |
| 379 | unit | George Douglas; Walker; Lt. Allen | George's furlough number 13; 'Now that Walker is in command, perhaps more men will be furloughed'; Lt. Allen reported shot on the 5th | Aug 21 1864 | 1864-08-21 | 06-104-P02 | I suppose you were so badly disappointed is the reason you said nothing about drawing for a furlough. If it is number 13. I want to know something about when your time will come to get off. ... Now that Walker is in command, perhaps more men will be furloughed. The paper said Lt. Allen was shot on the 5th but as you said nothing about it in yours of the 7th I suppose it is not true. |
| 380 | person | Ruth ('sisters black girl'); May; Sam; Mr [[Grames?]]; Mr Grounds; Cousin Dock; Ed | Ruth, sister's black girl, hired for two or three months; socks for Cousin Dock by Ed | Aug 21 1864 | 1864-08-21 | 06-104-P02; 06-104-P03 | Mother and Sue have gone to Mr [[Grames?]] Father to Mr Grounds to take some socks to send to Cousin Dock by Ed. Laura did not come home so I am here by myself, except Ruth, sisters black girl whom we have hired for two or three months. I must go down and stay with May to day ... I hope Sam will get your letter at the P O to day |
| 381 | relationship | Gail Douglas; Jesse Douglas; Sarah Gale Douglas; Eveline Gale (Manly) Douglas | Slips: Letter no. 9, April 30, 1864: death by childbirth of Gail Douglas, wife of Jesse Douglas; second slip: birth of Jesse's daughter Sarah Gale Douglas and death of her mother Eveline Gale (Manly) Douglas, April 23, 1864 | April 23, 1864 | 1864-04-23 | 06-105-P01; 06-105-P02 | — Tells of the death by / Childbirth of Gail Douglas, / wife of Jesse Douglas. ... AUNT LIZZIE TO UNCLE GEO. IN ARMY / REGARDING BIRTH OF JESSE'S DAUGHTER, / SARAH GALE DOUGLAS, AND DEATH OF HER / MOTHER EVELINE GALE (MANLY) DOUGLAS, / APRIL 23, 1864. |
| 382 | event | Jeppe; Gail; Mother; Sue; Henry; Hack | Jeppe again a widower; Gail died Saturday 23 [April 1864]; Mother brought the baby home; Jeppe would give it to Sue and Lizzie; 'Jeppe is fated to a life of trouble'; Henry brought Mother home from Hack | April 30th 1864 | 1864-04-30 | 06-106-P02 | I have sad news to communicate this time; Jeppe is again a widower. Gail died last Saturday (23d). Mother got home yesterday and brought the little baby with her. It is the poorest least one I ever saw ... Mother says Jeppe told her he would give it to Sue and me, poor little orphan, we will all do all we can for it. It seems that Jeppe is fated to a life of trouble. Henry brought Mother home, and left all the balance well on Hack. |
| 383 | church | Bro Jones; Marshall (Methodist preacher, Ark renegade); Parsons Austin and Haynes; Mary Freeman; Mary Gram | Bro Jones preached three sermons; Austin 'honestly differed on a few points'; Jones met Mr. Stone and preached for the soldiers when they left Dallas | April 1864 | 1864-04 | 06-106-P03; 06-107-P01 | Bro Jones preached three very good sermons. ... he is a methodist preacher, and [[inserted: an]] Ark renegade his name is Marshall. Parsons Austin and Haynes were both there. ... Austin made a few remarks after Jones got through ... He did not court controversy or debate, but he honestly differed on a few points. ... Jones says when you all left Dallas, he [[illegible]] Mr. Stone and preached once for you somewhere on the road. |
| 384 | event | Joda; Fields; Tarrant; Roxana; Mansfield; Sir; Wax.; Bow; Milligan | Joda got transportation (ox train) from Fields to haul to Milligan; things sent by way of Waxahachie | April 1864 | 1864-04 | 06-107-P02 | Joda after some difficulty has succeeded in getting transportation. Fields did not like to grant it, but at last consented ... Joda has now gone to Tarrant to have the things brought down Roxana to Mansfield; he said he would get Sir to come by Wax. so we could send your things over. ... We are very sorry he has to take an ox train as it will take so long to get to Milligan; but he could do no better as Fields positively refused to let him have a mule train. |
| 385 | relationship | Lizzie Douglas; George Douglas (Galveston) | Slip: Sept. 6, 1864, Letter no. 16, to George Douglas, Galveston; discussion not to hold union church meetings; Fayette & Jim prisoners, entire brigade captured | Sept. 6, 1864 | 1864-09-06 | 06-108-P01 | To George Douglas / Galveston, Texas. / from Sister / Lizzie Douglas / Brushy Creek / Sept. 6, 1864 / Letter no. 16 / — Mentions discussion / not to hold union / Church meetings / in community / — Speaks of Fayette & / Jim (Orr) being / held as prisoners / entire brigade captured |
| 386 | person | Mr. Collins; Lou; Frances Blair; Thomas | George's letter of 10 Aug and knife by Mr. Collins; letter from Frances Blair; Thomas not yet in the service | Sept. 6th 1864 | 1864-09-06 | 06-109-P02 | Last thursday I went up to the Office, and got yours of 10th ult. also the knife you sent by Mr. Collins ... I also received one from Frances Blair, who by the way is as good at letter writing, as conversation ... Thomas has not yet gone into the service. |
| 387 | event | Jeppe; Henry (Dr); Daniel H. Willbanks; Sallie Willbanks; Dr. Orr | Jeppe arrived from Hack Creek; Henry lost two patients incl. Daniel H. Willbanks whom Warren knew; Sallie Willbanks married Dr. Orr and they have parted; Jeppe made 130 bushels of corn | Sept 1864 | 1864-09 | 06-109-P03; 06-109-P04 | Jeppe arrived this evening. He brings no news, left all well on Hack Creek ... Henry has had a gooddeal of practice of late; he has lost two patients, one of them was Daniel H. Willbanks, Warren [[deleted: knows]] [[inserted: knew]] him. Sallie Willbanks married Dr. Orr, and they have parted. / Jeppe gathered his corn before he came down, made a hundred and thirty bushels. |
| 388 | event | Nancy Hancock (Stout); her brother; Mr. Telly; J. Bell | Sudden deaths of a strange disease; Mr. Telly's arm mortified; J. Bell suffering from his wound, ball working in his shoulder | Sept 1864 | 1864-09 | 06-109-P04; 06-110-P01 | Nancy Hancock, (Stout that was) was taken sick on Sunday and died on the next wednesday. ... her little brother was taken suddenly, and died [[deleted: illegible]] in two hours ... Mr. Telly — Nancy [[Culphers?]] husband — has been in bad health for a long time: on last Sunday morning a small red spot made its appearance on his arm ... mortification took place and he died on monday. ... J. Bell is now suffering severely from his wound. His shoulder is greatly swelled, and there is a place six inches long, in which he says he can feel the ball working back and forth. |
| 389 | money | Father; Mr. Martin; old Toby | Father sold old Toby to Mr. Martin for twenty three dollars in specie | Sept 1864 | 1864-09 | 06-109-P05 | Father has sold old Toby to Mr. Martin, for twenty three dollars in specie. |
| 390 | event | Mr. L. Evans; Sabine; Magruder | Evans left Sabine on the 31st [Aug 1864], seven days on the road; George's furlough not before Feb; 'I am glad old Magruder is gone' | Wednesday evening, 7th [Sept 1864] | 1864-09-07 | 06-110-P02; 06-110-P03 | Mr. L. Evans called on us, and left the books you sent, also the letter from Joda. ... says he left Sabine on the 31st thats only seven days on the road. ... Mr Evans says your time will not come before Feb. he also says you could get leave of absence the same as he did. ... I am glad old Magruder is gone. |
| 391 | event | The little baby (Sarah) | Five months old, weighs nine pounds | Sept 1864 | 1864-09 | 06-110-P04 | The little baby is five months old and weighs nine pounds. |
| 392 | church | Meetings at Brushy; Parson Hill; Mr. [[Batakers?]]; Bro Jones; Parson Austin; Milton Rawlins; Sarah Selby; Lizzie [[Burnard?]] | Protracted meeting; Methodists appointed a rival meeting; 'It is only the methodists that are so hostile. the presbyterians are not so afraid of the truth'; four united with the church; Bro Jones's sermon on the unity of the church | Sept 1864 | 1864-09 | 06-111-P01; 06-111-P02 | Every one preaches his own doctrine with all his might, consequently discussion [[inserted: is]] rife. ... so after studying on it awhile they appointed a meeting in Mr. [[Batakers?]] neighborhood embracing the same time! ... The saying of Parson Hill, "now if me and my neighbor dont agree I just let him alone, dont go about him," had sunk deep in their hearts, so union meetings were forgotten. ... It is only the methodists that are so hostile. the presbyterians are not so afraid of the truth. / Bro Jones was [[inserted: the]] only preacher from a distance ... There were no conversions, but four united with the church — Milton Rawlins, his wife, Sarah Selby, and Lizzie [[Burnard?]]. The meeting closed on monday night at which time Bro Jones preached one of the best sermons I ever heard on the unity of the church. |
| 393 | church | Parson Austin; Bro Goodman | Austin: 'they (the christians) have the bulge on us'; next protracted meeting in October | Sept 1864 | 1864-09 | 06-112-P01 | In his own language he says "I tell my brethren that they (the christians) have the bulge on us and we had better let them alone". ... an other protracted meeting was appointed for the [[deleted: illegible]] beginning friday night before the first Sunday in Oct. Bro Goodman told us several preachers would be there |
| 394 | event | Parson Austin; Lis. Gibbs; widow Mc Knight; Fayett and Jim; Mary Morgan; Mr. Phipp; Walk Hatter | Austin courting two weeks after his wife's death; Fayett and Jim captured or killed, whole brigade captured; Mary Morgan to marry Mr. Phipp; Walk Hatter a prisoner four months | Sept 1864 | 1864-09 | 06-112-P02 | Parson Austin was [[deleted: illegible]] waiting on Lis. Gibbs last Sunday, and [[gave?]] Saturday night. Dont you think that is right early? his wife has only been dead a little over two weeks. Father has just returned from the widow Mc Knights; he went down to get an auger to bore for water. He brings two items of news; one is that Fayett and Jim have either been taken prisoners or killed. A letter has been recd stating the whole Brigade was captured. ... Mary Morgan is to be married to night to Mr. [[deleted: illegible]] Phipp. ... I saw Walk Hatter the other day ... He was a prisoner four months, and says he fared well while in N's hands. He says the Soldiers he talked with sayed the would fight us ten years but what they would whip us; that old Abe gave them plenty to eat and wear |
| 395 | relationship | Lizzie Douglas; George Douglas (Sabine Pass); Mary Andrews; Rob Orr; Norvell Winneford | Slip: Aug. 5, 1864, Letter no 15, to George Douglas, Sabine Pass; party attended by Mary Andrews & Rob Orr; complains of Norvell Winneford working as teamster instead of serving | Aug. 5, 1864 | 1864-08-05 | 06-113-P01 | To George Douglas / Sabine Pass / from / Lizzie Douglas / Brushy Creek / Aug. 5, 1864 / Letter no 15 / — Tells of party attended / by Mary Andrews & / Rob Orr / — Complains of Norvell / Winneford working as / teamster instead of / serving |
| 396 | unit | George Douglas | George [moved] in the Reg.; P. H. married 'while the field was clear' | Aug 5th 64 | 1864-08-05 | 06-114-P02 | Though I am sorry to hear through Joda that you have been [[illegible]] in the Reg. You seemed to be getting along so well in the [[illegible]] I almost wished you could remain there. You seem to think P. H's chance of winning a fair one was rather bad, and that he had to marry while the field was clear. |
| 397 | person | Ed Dickson; uncle Graves; Joe; Laura; Em; Bee; May; Mary Orr; Sue; Bob Andrews | Candy pulling at uncle Graves's; young people named | Aug 1864 | 1864-08 | 06-115-P01 | Ed Dickson. He has got a furlough and is on a visit to this neighborhood now; he came over to invite us to a candy [[pulling?]] at his uncle graves's given in honor [[illegible]] who was to start back to the army Wednesday. ... Laura and Joe went over ... Em's, Bee, May and [[illegible]] were there, [[illegible]] and Mary Orr were the only [[illegible]] ... Sue, Bee, Joe [[illegible]], Bob Andrews Joe and "Ed the genus." |
| 398 | event | Election, Aug 1864: Frank Hawkins; Motes; Mr Love (Bay's Father, Mal. married); [[Bib?]] Hawkins; [[Tom?]] [[Coltham?]]; [[Norvell?]]; Abb; uncle Jim | Frank Hawkins re-elected clerk; Love 130, Coltham 159; Norvell holds two offices, enrolling officer and general of the militia; a man of 50 enrolled in the militia threatens suit for false imprisonment | last monday [Aug 1864] | 1864-08-01 | 06-115-P02 | last monday was election day. The result is this, Frank Hawkins was elected again for city clerk. His opponents was named Motes Mr Love, (Bay's Father and the same one Mal. married). [[Bib?]] Hawkins, [[Tom?]] [[Coltham?]] and [[Mr?]] [[illegible]] were the candidates for [[Circuit?]] Clerk ... He only came out about a week before the election & got only ninety votes. Love 130, Coltham 159. [[Norvell?]] will now live [[illegible]], but he already holds two offices—enrolling officer, and general of the militia. ... he protested against serving any longer than till he was fifty. He now says, he is going to stay at home till [[illegible]] after him, and then he is going to sue for false imprisonment |
| 399 | church | Em Gibbs; Bro Goodman; Cedar Hill | 'worship the Lord next Sunday in our dirty clothes' joke; Bro Goodman's literary discourse; Methodist big meeting near Cedar Hill | Saturday 6th [Aug 1864] | 1864-08-06 | 06-116-P01; 06-116-P02 | We agreed that we did not wish to "worship the Lord next Sunday in our dirty clothes" ha ha. ... This morning Mother, Laura and I went over to [[Lis's?]] [[illegible]] Bro Goodman preached [[illegible]] a literary [[illegible]] discourse. ... They have now [[illegible]] at [[illegible]] somewhere near Cedar Hill. |
| 400 | event | Steel[e]; Kentuckey Town; [[Chisol?]]; the Guardian | Rumour of whipping Steele in Arkansas and capturing two hundred wagons; seven men hung near Kentucky Town | [spring 1864] | 1864 | 06-117-P01 | It is rumour / [[lost: torn edge]] here that we have whipt Steel / in Ars and captured two hundd / waggons. Seven men were recent / ly hung near Kentuckey Town / [[lost: torn edge]] one of these named [[Chisol?]] |
| 401 | event | Robertson; Z. [[Tackett?]]; [[Old?]] man [[Crum?]]; Jeff Davis | Teamsters keeping out of the army: 'got to drive his waggons! getting biger pay than any of us' |  |  | 06-118-P01 | [[illegible]] has succeeded in / getting in as a teamster [[illegible]] Z. / [[Tackett?]] has got in to drive one of / R's teams. ... so you see good luck / for Robertson. ... to see Jeff Davis! got to drive / his waggons! getting biger pay / than any of us"! |

## C. Answers to the open questions, from the papers only

### C.1 Nathaniel Lynn Douglas

**Name and form.** The manuscripts never spell out "Nathaniel Lynn". The full given name appears only in the repository's scope note ("the day book of Nathaniel Lynn Douglas", 01-003-P09) and in James Bass's address panel, "Nathaniel Douglas / Red Oak Post Office / Texas" (05-015-P02). Everywhere else he is "N L Douglas" (05-012-P02, 05-021-P01, 05-025-P01), "N. L. Douglas" (04-042-P02, 05-151-P01), once "L. N. Douglas" on a later slip (05-013-P01), "Father" (Henry's letters, 04-079-P01; Lizzie's letters, 06-090-P01) and "Mr. N. L. Douglas" on a slip (04-078-P01).

**Birthplace, parents, siblings.** The papers are silent. No document names his birthplace, his father, his mother or any sibling. The repository's own review note in this edition already records that his father "has not been established by this project" (REVIEW-STATUS.md). "Jesse Douglass of the Waxhaws" and "Susannah" do not occur anywhere in the supplied scans; the Jesse and Susannah who do occur are his children (Jeppe/Jesse, 04-042-P03; "Susannah", 05-006-P01). Neither South Carolina nor Darlington nor Lexington is mentioned in any document.

**Tennessee years.** The only direct link between N. L. Douglas and Murfreesboro is the finding aid's description of the day book: "The Daybook of N. L. Douglas of Murfreesboro TN and Ellis County, Liberty Settlement, Red Oak TX beginning Jan. 4, 1830" (01-006-P03). The two earliest instruments in the supplied scans are consistent with a Tennessee residence: a due bill "Due N. L. Douglas or bearer five dollars value received. January. 21st 1829. / [[S W?]] Webber" (05-022-P02) and Samuel Wallace's note "By the 25th December next I promise to pay to N L Douglas at his house five barrels corn, or four Dollars, value recd this 2nd Oct. 1832" (05-012-P02). Neither names a place. His later Tennessee connections are all with Rutherford County: Geo A Sublett "of the County of Rutherford and State of Tennessee" calls him "my friend N L Douglas of Ellis County" in 1852 (05-025-P01); James Bass writes from Murfreesboro in 1854 about "our business" of "nearly three years" (05-014-P01); Henry reports Mr Mallard "bound for old Rutherford" (04-081-P01); the 1883 inheritance letters come from Murfreesboro lawyers and cousins.

**Marriage to Mary "Polly" Goodloe.** No marriage record or date is in the papers. Mary is identified as his wife by the estate inventory, "Mary Douglas relict of deceased" (05-151-P01), by the heirs' quitclaim "deeded by Mary Douglas our mother" (04-042-P02), by Henry's letters to "Dear Mother" addressed "Mrs Mary Douglas, Red Oak Texas" (04-093-P01, 04-096-P03), and by the slip "To Mrs Mary (N. L.) Douglas" (04-092-P01). Her Goodloe birth is stated only by later slips and by inference from the lawyers' letters: the slip on Lillard's letter reads "To Mrs. (Mary) Douglas & Cynthia Orr – daughters of Rebecca Goodloe" (05-086-P01); Lillard tells Mary that Mrs Alexander was "daughter of your uncle Jacob Wright" (05-095-P01), which makes Mary's mother a Wright; the Fannie slip calls Newton Goodloe "the brother of Mary Douglas & Cynthia Orr" (06-026-P01); and the lawyers count "3 of the Goodloe family" among the first cousins (05-095-P01). The name "Polly" is never used of her in the papers (the only Polly is "Old Aunt Polly Wright", 05-120-P01). Her birth date and birthplace are not given.

**Trade.** The papers are silent on tailoring. The only occupational evidence is indirect: he acts as land agent and attorney-in-fact for Sublett (05-021, 05-025, 05-033), holds promissory notes (05-012, 05-017), receives a business letter from Bass about seeing to a 3,250-acre tract and its taxes (05-014-P01), and farms (the 1874 inventory: 94 acres in cultivation, 9 horses, 20 cattle, 14 hogs, 20 bee hives, 05-151-P02). Lizzie's wartime letters show "Father" buying a sow and shoats for specie, reserving twenty acres for corn, having wheat raised on shares, selling a horse for $23 specie and boring for water (06-090-P01, 06-109-P05, 06-112-P02).

**Arrival in Texas.** No document states the year. The earliest Texas evidence is his agency for Sublett in redeeming Navarro County land: the certificate of 23 January 1851 recites a tax sale of "the fourth day of February AD 1850" (05-021-P01), and the 1852 power of attorney calls him "of Ellis County State of Texas" (05-025-P01). Henry's letter of 8 September 1851 says Anderson County has been sicklier "than I ever saw it in Ellis Co." (04-079-P01), and the letter of April 1853 recalls, as a memory already old, the day Henry "parted from Father, in the wide lonely prairie, near the Willow pond" and walked to Porters Bluff (05-007-P01). Bass's April 1854 letter says it has been "nearly three years" since he heard from Douglas about their business (05-014-P01), and "Nathaniel Douglas, Red Oak Post Office" is his address by then (05-015-P02). None of the competing dates (1846, 1848, 1849) is confirmed or excluded; the papers show him established in Ellis County by early 1851 at the latest. Inference: Sublett's Porter-survey tract, sold for the 1848 taxes and redeemed through Douglas in 1851 (05-021-P01), is the same 1,280-acre A. J. Porter survey on Sublett's Creek of which Douglas's estate held 640 acres in 1874 (05-151-P02); how and when he acquired it is not in the papers.

**The day book.** Not supplied. Everything known about it comes from two lines of the finding aid: "the day book of Nathaniel Lynn Douglas, beginning in 1830" (01-003-P09) and "The Daybook of N. L. Douglas of Murfreesboro TN and Ellis County, Liberty Settlement, Red Oak TX beginning Jan. 4, 1830 / Business correspondence of N. L. Douglas" (01-006-P03), in Folder 5. Its contents (accounts, customers, births, travel) cannot be determined from the supplied scans.

**Death.** The papers give no death date. The inventory of his estate was sworn 5 October 1874 (05-151-P03; the slip reads 3 October, 05-150-P01), the heirs quitclaimed 19 February 1875 (04-042-P03), and Mary deeded land to Lizzie on 25 February 1875 (05-157-P01). Henry's letter of 13 March 1865 condoles George on "the Death of your Sister" (04-010-P04), not a parent. The date 12 April 1874 is consistent with, but not stated by, the papers.

### C.2 Slavery

**Ownership, hiring, trading.** No bill of sale, hire contract, tax list or estate entry for an enslaved person appears in the supplied scans. The 1874 inventory of N. L. Douglas's estate lists land, horses, cattle, hogs, bee hives and tools only (05-151-P02), but it postdates emancipation and so is not evidence either way for the antebellum household. The only references to enslaved or Black people are these, all in letters:

- Lizzie, 28 June 1864, on a neighbour's marriage: "Old man Durst and a widow Smith of Johnson Co; It is reported she has a hundred negroes. All I can say is [[illegible]] her." (06-098-P03)
- Lizzie, 21 August 1864: "Laura did not come home so I am here by myself, except Ruth, sisters black girl whom we have hired for two or three months." (06-104-P02). "Sisters" is Sue Bell (see C.6). This is the one statement that a household of the family hired a Black servant, in 1864; her legal status and the terms are not stated.
- L. A. Jetton in Tennessee, 20 March 1883: "I have Such a lonely time here only a little negro with me" (06-014-P01).
- Lizzie Graves in Alabama, 29 August 1867, enclosing a letter for "Jane and Mary": "give it to any of or all the boys and they will get some person to read it for them" (06-050-P02).
- Lizzie, 6 August 1864, reporting a sermon, "of an old negro getting [[religion?]]" (06-116-P02); Lizzie Graves, May 1867, "The colored ladies of the city of Montgomery" decorating the Federal graves (06-054-P01); George in 1923, "White folks wont work … and niggers are scarce" (04-069-P01).

**Tax assessor-collector.** The papers are silent on any office held by Nathaniel or George. The only assessor-collector in the papers is W. B. McCabe of Navarro County (05-021-P01, 05-033-P05); N. L. Douglas appears before him as a private agent. No tax roll, assessment or bond is present. (The 1923 letters mention George's own "tax receipt" and a pending "tax business" suit, 04-073-P01, 04-074-P01; that is about his taxes as a taxpayer.)

### C.3 George Douglas

**Birth date and place.** The papers are silent. No document gives George's birth date, age or birthplace. The closest is Bob's 1863 letter recalling boyhood on "old Brushy" (04-019-P04) and George's own words in 1923, "With the number of my years I cant stay here long" (04-053-P01).

**Wife and children.** No marriage is recorded. The papers show him a widower or single householder in the undated Zella letters ("batching", "my next cook must look refined", 04-034-P04). His children by name: **Zella Douglas** (addressed at 1715 Grand Avenue, North Fort Worth, 04-025-P02, and at Red Oak, 04-055-P01; "Miss Zella Douglas property", 04-046-P03; payee with Bruce Headrick on the 1939 loans, 04-040-P07, 04-041-P07) is "My Dear Child" to "Papa" (04-048-P02, 04-052-P04), and George tells her "you are all I have that is best to live for" (04-053-P01). The memoir fragment "My father continued his labors with the Church at Sylvana until his death Sept 23 1923" (05-062-P01) is by an unnamed child; the slip calls him "Uncle George" (05-060-P01), so the slip-writer was a niece or nephew, not the author. **Kate** (with Clarence) is a niece: "My nieces cant understand why I persist in staying at home" (04-034-P04) alongside repeated visits "At Kate's" (04-029-P04, 04-035-P04); Henry's 1892 letter shows Clarence and Kate as Henry's children, twins born 8 September 1878 ("They will be 14 the 8th inst", 04-089-P01). **C. C. Douglas** of 212 East University, Waxahachie, receives a New York envelope in 1941 (04-039-P02); his relation to George is not stated (inference: possibly Clarence, Henry's son). No other child of George is named.

**Civil War unit and rank.** No muster, commission or discharge is in the scans. The evidence is: the address panel "George Douglas / Co C / [[illegible]] Regt / Sab Pass" of June 1864 (04-003-P01; the family edition reads the regiment as "6th Texas", unconfirmed); Lizzie's remark in February 1864 that Joda "seems to think your Batt. is disgraced worse than any other body of men in the Army" (06-094-P03); slips addressing him at "Sabine Pass" (06-113-P01) and "Galveston, Texas" (06-108-P01); "you have been [[illegible]] in the Reg." (06-114-P02, August 1864); George "drawing for a furlough … If it is number 13" and "Now that Walker is in command, perhaps more men will be furloughed" (06-104-P02, August 1864); Mr. L. Evans "left Sabine on the 31st" of August 1864 carrying George's books (06-110-P02); "your return from Velasco" in April 1865 (06-069-P02); the finding aid's "George Douglas served in the Confederate Army during the Civil War" (01-003-P10). He was writing from camp by 22 January 1863 at the latest, the date of the earliest letter to him listed in Folder 6 (01-006-P04). No rank is ever given; his sisters and cousins never call him anything but George or "Bro." The word "private" does not occur. Inference (from outside the papers, to be verified): the Sabine Pass garrison of 1864 that furloughed by lot and was affected by "Griffin" (06-091-P02) points to the 21st Texas Infantry Battalion/Regiment (Griffin's and Spaight's); the papers themselves do not name it.

**The journal and the battle of Sabine Pass.** The journal is not among the supplied scans.
